(i,j)
is the index of the
i
-th row and
j
-th column.

3.
Anderson
t
-modules

The theory of Anderson
t
-modules over Tate algebras was initiated by Anglès, Pellarin, and Tavares Ribeiro
[
11
,
8
]
and continued in several articles including
[
9
,
7
,
13
,
35
,
36
,
33
,
34
,
70
]
. They also are the main objects of study of Demeslay
[
24
,
25
]
for his class module formula, which we recount in §
5.3
. We will follow Demeslay’s definition of Anderson
t
-modules in
[
24
]
, but in order to discuss reductions modulo irreducible elements of
A
we will require a slightly more general construction over “
\mathcal{A}
-fields.”

3.1.
Anderson
t
-modules over
\mathcal{A}
-fields

Throughout §
3
we will consider a more general situation, which will help streamline our arguments and may be of benefit for future work. We let
Z\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\{z_{1},\dots,z_{n}\}
, for variables
z_{1},\dots,z_{n}
and for
n\geqslant 0
(so
n=0\Leftrightarrow Z=\emptyset
). Then for a field
F
we let

{F\langle Z\rangle}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=F(Z)\quad\textup{or}\quad{F(\!(Z)\!)},

so
{F\langle Z\rangle}
denotes either a field of rational functions or formal Laurent series.
We thus define fields
{\mathbb{F}_{q}\langle Z\rangle}\subseteq{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}\langle Z\rangle}
, and note that
{\mathbb{F}_{q}\langle Z\rangle}\cap\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}=\mathbb{F}_{q}
. We let

\mathcal{A}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}={\mathbb{F}_{q}\langle Z\rangle}[t].

For the purposes of this paper, we will use primarily
Z=\emptyset
or
Z=\{z\}
, which has prompted us to combine these cases into a single exposition, but the cases of
Z=\{z_{1},\dots,z_{n}\}
for
n\geqslant 2
may be of future interest.

Definition 3.1.1
.

Given an
\mathsf{A}
-field
F
and an
{\mathbb{F}_{q}\langle Z\rangle}
-algebra
H\supseteq F
, we extend
\iota:\mathsf{A}\to F
to
\iota:\mathcal{A}\to H
by composing the natural maps

\iota:\mathcal{A}={\mathbb{F}_{q}\langle Z\rangle}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}\mathsf{A}\xrightarrow{\id\otimes\iota}{\mathbb{F}_{q}\langle Z\rangle}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}F\xrightarrow{a\otimes b\,\mapsto\,ab}H.

We assume that we have an extension
\tau:H\to H
that is
{\mathbb{F}_{q}\langle Z\rangle}
-linear. We further assume that
H^{\tau}={\mathbb{F}_{q}\langle Z\rangle}
, where
H^{\tau}
denotes the elements of
H
fixed by
\tau
. If
\tau:H\to H
is an automorphism, we say
H
is
\tau
-perfect
, and if
H
is a field, we call it an
\mathcal{A}
-field
. Primarily we will be interested in
\tau
-perfect
\mathcal{A}
-fields. The
characteristic
of
H
is
\ker\iota\subseteq\mathcal{A}
, and as in the case of
\mathsf{A}
-fields, the characteristic is generic if
\ker\iota=(0)
and finite otherwise.

One advantage to this framework is that we can discuss objects over
\mathsf{A}
-fields (where
{\mathbb{F}_{q}\langle Z\rangle}=\mathbb{F}_{q}
) and
\mathbf{A}
-fields (where
{\mathbb{F}_{q}\langle Z\rangle}=\mathbb{F}_{q}(z)
) simultaneously. We note that
\tau
-perfect
\mathsf{A}
-fields are the same as perfect
\mathsf{A}
-fields.

Let
H
be an
\mathcal{A}
-field. An
Anderson
t
-module over
H
(or simply
t
-module over
H
) is an
{\mathbb{F}_{q}\langle Z\rangle}
-algebra homomorphism,

\mathcal{E}:\mathcal{A}\to\Mat_{\ell}(H[\tau])

such that if

(3.1.2)

\mathcal{E}_{t}=\partial\mathcal{E}_{t}+E_{1}\tau+\cdots+E_{w}\tau^{w},\quad E_{i}\in\Mat_{\ell}(H),

then
\partial\mathcal{E}_{t}-\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5mu\theta}\mathrm{I}_{\ell}
is nilpotent. As in §
2.1
,
\mathcal{E}
defines an
\mathcal{A}
-module structure on
\mathcal{E}(H)=H^{\ell}
through the operation of
\tau
, and correspondingly we have
\Lie(\mathcal{E})(H)=H^{\ell}
with
H[t]
-module structure induced by
\partial\mathcal{E}_{a}
for
a\in\mathcal{A}
. If
\ell=1
, then
\mathcal{E}
is a
Drinfeld module over
H
. If
\mathcal{E}_{a}\in\Mat_{\ell}(F[\tau])
for all
a\in\mathcal{A}
(equivalently simply
\mathcal{E}_{t}\in\Mat_{\ell}(F[\tau])
), then
\mathcal{E}
is a
constant
Anderson
t
-module with respect to
H/F
.

If
H
is a
\tau
-perfect
\mathcal{A}
-field, the
adjoint
of
\mathcal{E}
is defined by the
{\mathbb{F}_{q}\langle Z\rangle}
-algebra map,

\mathcal{E}^{*}:\mathcal{A}\to\Mat_{\ell}(H[\sigma]),

where

(3.1.3)

\mathcal{E}^{*}_{t}=(\mathcal{E}_{t})^{*}=(\partial\mathcal{E}_{t})^{{\mathsf{T}}}+\bigl(E_{1}^{(-1)}\bigr)^{{\mathsf{T}}}\sigma+\cdots+\bigl(E_{w}^{(-w)}\bigr)^{{\mathsf{T}}}\sigma^{w}.

Similarly for adjoints of
t
-modules over perfect
\mathsf{A}
-fields,
\mathcal{E}^{*}
defines an
\mathcal{A}
-module structure on
\mathcal{E}^{*}(H)=H^{\ell}
.
As in §
2.2
, if
\mathcal{D}:\mathcal{A}\to\Mat_{k}(H[\tau])
is another
t
-module over
H
, then
\eta\in\Mat_{\ell\times k}(H[\tau])
is a morphism
\eta:\mathcal{D}\to\mathcal{E}
of
t
-modules over
H
if

\eta\cdot\mathcal{E}_{a}=\mathcal{D}_{a}\cdot\eta
, for all
a\in\mathcal{A}
.
Correspondingly,
\eta^{*}:\mathcal{E}^{*}\to\mathcal{D}^{*}
is a morphism of their adjoints.

3.2.
t
-motives and dual
t
-motives for Anderson
t
-modules

We extend the notions of
t
-motives and dual
t
-motives from §
2.4
to this setting. We start with a
\tau
-perfect
\mathcal{A}
-field
H
, and let
\mathcal{E}:\mathcal{A}\to\Mat_{\ell}(H[\tau])
be a
t
-module over
H
defined as in (
3.1.2
).

The
t
-motive
of
\mathcal{E}
is defined to be
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{E}}=\Mat_{1\times\ell}(H[\tau])
, which as in §
2.4
is given the structure of a left
H[t,\tau]
-module by setting
a\cdot m=m\mathcal{E}_{a}
for any
m\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{E}}
,
a\in\mathcal{A}
. If needed we will write
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{E}}(H)=\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{E}}
to emphasize the dependence on
H
. We say
\mathcal{E}
and
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{E}}
are
abelian
and have
rank
r
if
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{E}}
is free and rank
r
as an
H[t]
-module.

The
dual
t
-motive
of
\mathcal{E}
is defined to be
\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{E}}=\Mat_{1\times\ell}(H[\sigma])
, which as in §
2.4
, is given the structure of a left
H[t,\sigma]
-module by setting
a\cdot n=n\mathcal{E}_{a}^{*}
for any
n\in\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{E}}
,
a\in\mathcal{A}
. Dual
t
-motives over Tate algebras were previously studied by Demeslay
[
25
]
*§1.2. If needed we will write
\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{E}}(H)=\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{E}}
to emphasize the dependence on
H
. We say
\mathcal{E}
and
\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{E}}
are
\mathcal{A}
-finite
and have
rank
r
if
\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{E}}
is free and rank
r
as an
H[t]
-module. It is not yet known if abelian and
\mathcal{A}
-finite are equivalent for
t
-modules over general
H
, though it would be interesting to investigate how the work of Maurischat
[
52
]
applies here.

The dual
t
-motive of a traditional
t
-module over a perfect
\mathsf{A}
-field can be used to recover the
t
-module (e.g., see
[
15
]
*§1.5
[
47
]
*Prop. 2.5.8
[
54
]
*§3.1), and one has a similar construction for Drinfeld modules over Tate algebras
[
33
]
*Lem. 4.2.2. The connection between
t
-modules and dual
t
-motives over
\tau
-perfect
\mathcal{A}
-fields is similar, and furthermore, we can relate the adjoint of a
t
-module with its
t
-motive.

Define maps
\gamma_{0}
,
\gamma_{1}:\Mat_{1\times\ell}(H[\tau])\to H^{\ell}
and
\delta_{0}
,
\delta_{1}:\Mat_{1\times\ell}(H[\sigma])\to H^{\ell}
by

\gamma_{0}(m)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\partial m^{{\mathsf{T}}}=c_{0}^{{\mathsf{T}}},\quad\gamma_{1}(m)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\sum_{i=0}^{i_{0}}\Bigl(c_{i}^{(-i)}\Bigr)^{{\mathsf{T}}},\quad\forall\,m=\sum_{i=0}^{i_{0}}c_{i}\tau^{i}\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{E}}(H),

and

\delta_{0}(n)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\partial n^{{\mathsf{T}}}=d_{0}^{{\mathsf{T}}},\quad\delta_{1}(n)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\sum_{i=0}^{i_{0}}\Bigl(d_{i}^{(i)}\Bigr)^{{\mathsf{T}}},\quad\forall\,n=\sum_{i=0}^{i_{0}}d_{i}\sigma^{i}\in\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{E}}(H).

Both
\gamma_{0}
,
\delta_{0}
are
H
-linear, while
\gamma_{1}
,
\delta_{1}
are
{\mathbb{F}_{q}\langle Z\rangle}
-linear (by assumption
H^{\tau}=H^{\sigma}={\mathbb{F}_{q}\langle Z\rangle}
).

Lemma 3.2.1
.

Let
H
be a
\tau
-perfect
\mathcal{A}
-field, and let
\mathcal{E}:\mathcal{A}\to\Mat_{\ell}(H[\tau])
be a
t
-module over
H
. As defined above the following hold.

(a)

Each of the maps
\gamma_{0}
,
\gamma_{1}:\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{E}}(H)\to H^{\ell}
and
\delta_{0}
,
\delta_{1}:\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{E}}(H)\to H^{\ell}
is surjective.

(b)

\ker\gamma_{0}=\tau\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{E}}(H)
and
\ker\delta_{0}=\sigma\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{E}}(H)
.

(c)

\ker\gamma_{1}=(\tau-1)\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{E}}(H)
and
\ker\delta_{1}=(\sigma-1)\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{E}}(H)
.

Proof.

Part (a) is clear, as are the statements about
\ker\gamma_{0}
and
\ker\delta_{0}
(though note that we use that
\tau
and
\sigma
are automorphisms of
H
). The arguments to determine
\ker\gamma_{1}
and
\ker\delta_{1}
are similar to the situation of traditional
t
-modules, but for completeness we include the argument for
\ker\gamma_{1}
. Showing
\ker\gamma_{1}\supseteq(\tau-1)\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{E}}(H)
is straightforward, and to show the opposite containment, we let
m=\sum_{i=0}^{i_{0}}c_{i}\tau^{i}\in\ker\gamma_{1}
. From the left division algorithm of §
2.1.13
we have
m^{\prime}
,
s\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{E}}(H)
so that
m=(\tau-1)m^{\prime}+s
.
Since
\ker\gamma_{1}\supseteq(\tau-1)\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{E}}(H)
we see that
\gamma_{1}(s)=0
. As
\gamma_{1}
is injective on
\Mat_{1\times\ell}(H)
, we conclude
s=0
.
∎

If
\mathcal{D}:\mathcal{A}\to\Mat_{k}(H[\tau])
is a
t
-module, and
\eta\in\Mat_{\ell\times k}(H[\tau])
represents a morphism
\eta:\mathcal{D}\to\mathcal{E}
, then the following diagrams of
H
-vector spaces have exact rows and commute:

(3.2.2)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{E}}(H)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{E}}(H)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{\ell}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{D}}(H)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{D}}(H)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau(\cdot)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\cdot)\eta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\cdot)\eta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial\eta^{{\mathsf{T}}}(\cdot)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau(\cdot)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{0}}

and

(3.2.3)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{D}}(H)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{D}}(H)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{E}}(H)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{E}}(H)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{\ell}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sigma(\cdot)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\cdot)\eta^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\cdot)\eta^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial\eta(\cdot)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sigma(\cdot)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta_{0}}

The first two vertical columns are simply
\eta^{\dagger}:\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{E}}\to\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{D}}
and
\eta^{\ddagger}:\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{D}}\to\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{E}}
. Furthermore, we have the following commutative diagrams of
{\mathbb{F}_{q}\langle Z\rangle}
-vector spaces with exact rows:

(3.2.4)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{E}}(H)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{E}}(H)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}^{*}(H)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{D}}(H)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{D}}(H)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}^{*}(H)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\tau-1)(\cdot)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\cdot)\eta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\cdot)\eta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta^{*}(\cdot)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\tau-1)(\cdot)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{1}}

and

(3.2.5)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{D}}(H)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{D}}(H)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}(H)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{E}}(H)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{E}}(H)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}(H)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\sigma-1)(\cdot)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\cdot)\eta^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\cdot)\eta^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta(\cdot)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\sigma-1)(\cdot)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta_{1}}

We summarize these findings in the following proposition.

Proposition 3.2.6
.

Let
H
be a
\tau
-perfect
\mathcal{A}
-field, and let
\mathcal{E}:\mathcal{A}\to\Mat_{\ell}(H[\tau])
be an Anderson
t
-module over
H
.

(a)

We have isomorphisms of
H[t]
-modules,

\frac{\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{E}}(H)}{\tau\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{E}}(H)}\cong\Lie(\mathcal{E}^{*})(H),\quad\frac{\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{E}}(H)}{\sigma\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{E}}(H)}\cong\Lie(\mathcal{E})(H).

(b)

We have isomorphisms of
\mathcal{A}
-modules,

\frac{\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{E}}(H)}{(\tau-1)\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{E}}(H)}\cong\mathcal{E}^{*}(H),\quad\frac{\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{E}}(H)}{(\sigma-1)\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{E}}(H)}\cong\mathcal{E}(H).

(c)

The isomorphisms in
(a)
and
(b)
are functorial in
\mathcal{E}
.

3.2.7.
Abelian and
\mathcal{A}
-finite
t
-modules

Assume
\mathcal{E}
is abelian and
\mathcal{A}
-finite and that both
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{E}}
and
\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{E}}
have rank
r
as
H[t]
-modules. We fix
H[t]
-bases
\mathbf{m}\in\Mat_{r\times 1}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{E}}(H))
and
\mathbf{n}\in\Mat_{r\times 1}(\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{E}}(H))
, and define
\Gamma
,
\Phi\in\Mat_{r}(H[t])
so that

\tau\mathbf{m}=\Gamma\mathbf{m}
and
\sigma\mathbf{n}=\Phi\mathbf{n}
.
As in the case of §
2.4
, the fact that
(\partial\mathcal{E}_{t}-\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5mu\theta}\mathrm{I}_{\ell})^{\ell}=0
implies that
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{E}}/\tau\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{E}}
and
\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{E}}/\sigma\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{E}}
are finite
H[t]
-modules annihilated by powers of
t-\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5mu\theta}
. As such we find that
\det\Gamma=c(t-\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5mu\theta})^{\ell}
and
\det\Phi=c^{\prime}(t-\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5mu\theta})^{\ell}
for some
c
,
c^{\prime}\in H^{\times}
.
Thus
{[\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{E}}/\tau\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{E}}]}_{H[t]}={[\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{E}}/\sigma\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{E}}]}_{H[t]}=(t-\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5mu\theta})^{\ell}
.

3.3.
Kernels of morphisms

Let
H
be a
\tau
-perfect
\mathcal{A}
-field. For
\eta\in\Mat_{\ell\times k}(H[\tau])
, we consider
{\mathbb{F}_{q}\langle Z\rangle}
-vector spaces,

(\ker\eta)(H)=\{\mathbf{x}\in H^{k}\mid\eta(\mathbf{x})=0\},\quad(\ker\eta^{*})(H)=\{\mathbf{x}\in H^{\ell}\mid\eta^{*}(\mathbf{x})=0\}.

We are particularly interested in the case when
\mathcal{D}:\mathcal{A}\to\Mat_{k}(H[\tau])
and
\mathcal{E}:\mathcal{A}\to\Mat_{\ell}(H[\tau])
are Anderson
t
-modules, and
\eta:\mathcal{D}\to\mathcal{E}
is a morphism. For
a\in\mathcal{A}
, we set

\mathcal{E}[a](H)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=(\ker\mathcal{E}_{a})(H),\quad\mathcal{E}^{*}[a](H)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=(\ker\mathcal{E}^{*}_{a})(H).

Lemma 3.3.1
.

Let
\eta\in\Mat_{\ell}(H[\tau])
be given by
\eta=N_{j}\tau^{j}+N_{j+1}\tau^{j+1}+\cdots+N_{m}\tau^{m}
, for
N_{i}\in\Mat_{\ell}(H)

such that
\det N_{m}\neq 0
. Then
(\ker\eta)(H)
and
(\ker\eta^{*})(H)
are
{\mathbb{F}_{q}\langle Z\rangle}
-vector spaces of dimension at most
(m-j)\ell
.

Proof.

Since
\tau
is an automorphism, it suffices to prove the case
j=0
.
The argument is essentially the same as
[
8
]
*Lem. 5.7 (cf.
[
72
]
*§1.2), but we sketch the main points. Let

M\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\begin{pmatrix}0&\mathrm{I}_{\ell}&\cdots&0\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
0&0&\cdots&\mathrm{I}_{\ell}\\
-N_{m}^{-1}N_{0}&-N_{m}^{-1}N_{1}&\cdots&-N_{m}^{-1}N_{m-1}\end{pmatrix}\in\Mat_{\ell m}(H).

For any
v_{1},\dots,v_{s}\in H^{\ell m}
such that
\tau(v_{i})=v_{i}^{(1)}=Mv_{i}
for each
i
, the following holds: if
v_{1},\dots,v_{s}
are
{\mathbb{F}_{q}\langle Z\rangle}
-linearly independent (
H^{\tau}={\mathbb{F}_{q}\langle Z\rangle}
), then they are are
H
-linearly independent (cf.
[
4
]
*pf. of Thm. 2
[
72
]
*pf. of Lem. 1.7). The map
\mathbf{x}\mapsto(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}^{(1)},\dots,\mathbf{x}^{(m-1)})^{{\mathsf{T}}}
is an
{\mathbb{F}_{q}\langle Z\rangle}
-linear isomorphism from
(\ker\eta)(H)
to
\{v\in H^{\ell m}\mid v^{(1)}=Mv\}
, which provides the desired conclusion. The argument for
(\ker\eta^{*})(H)
is similar.
∎

Let
\mathcal{E}:\mathcal{A}\to\Mat_{\ell}(H[\tau])
be an Anderson
t
-module over
H
, and let
\eta\in\Mat_{\ell}(H[\tau])
represent an endomorphism of
\mathcal{E}
. Applying the snake lemma to (
3.2.4
) and (
3.2.5
), we obtain exact sequences of
{\mathbb{F}_{q}\langle Z\rangle}
-modules,

(3.3.2)

0\to(\ker\eta^{*})(H)\to\frac{\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{E}}(H)}{\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{E}}(H)\eta}\xrightarrow{(\tau-1)(\cdot)}\frac{\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{E}}(H)}{\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{E}}(H)\eta}

and

(3.3.3)

0\to(\ker\eta)(H)\to\frac{\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{E}}(H)}{\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{E}}(H)\eta^{*}}\xrightarrow{(\sigma-1)(\cdot)}\frac{\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{E}}(H)}{\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{E}}(H)\eta^{*}}.

Proposition 3.3.4
.

Let
\mathcal{E}:\mathcal{A}\to\Mat_{\ell}(H[\tau])
be an Anderson
t
-module over
H
, and let
\eta:\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{E}
be an endomorphism given by
\eta\in\Mat_{\ell}(H[\tau])
with
\partial\eta\in\GL_{\ell}(H)
. Let
D=\diag(d_{1},\dots,d_{\ell})\in\Mat_{\ell}(H[\tau])
be a diagonal matrix associated to
\eta
as in Proposition
2.1.14
. Then none of
d_{1},\dots,d_{\ell}
is
0
, and

\dim_{{\mathbb{F}_{q}\langle Z\rangle}}(\ker\eta)(H)\leqslant\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}\deg_{\tau}d_{i},\quad\dim_{{\mathbb{F}_{q}\langle Z\rangle}}(\ker\eta^{*})(H)\leqslant\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}\deg_{\tau}d_{i}.

Proof.

Let
U
,
V\in\GL_{\ell}(H[\tau])
be chosen so that
D=U\eta V
. Then
\partial D=\partial U\cdot\partial\eta\cdot\partial V
. Since
\partial\eta
is invertible, so must be
\partial D
, which implies that none of
d_{1},\dots,d_{\ell}
is zero.

By (
3.3.2
), we have the isomorphism of
{\mathbb{F}_{q}\langle Z\rangle}
-vector spaces,

(3.3.5)

(\ker\eta^{*})(H)\cong J\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\ker\biggl(\frac{\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{E}}(H)}{\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{E}}(H)\eta}\xrightarrow{(\tau-1)(\cdot)}\frac{\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{E}}(H)}{\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{E}}(H)\eta}\biggr).

Suppose that
m_{1},\dots,m_{k}\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{E}}(H)
represent
{\mathbb{F}_{q}\langle Z\rangle}
-linearly independent classes in
J
. We claim that
m_{1},\dots,m_{k}
represent
H
-linearly independent classes in
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{E}}(H)/\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{E}}(H)\eta
. Since
m_{1},\dots,m_{k}\in J
, there are
\beta_{1},\dots,\beta_{k}\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{E}}(H)
so that
(\tau-1)m_{i}=\beta_{i}\eta
for each
i
. Suppose that
m_{1},\dots,m_{k}
are
H
-linearly dependent modulo
\mathcal{M}_{E}(H)\eta
, so after reordering terms, we can choose
j\leqslant k
minimal with
c_{2},\dots,c_{j}\in H
and
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{E}}(H)
so that

(3.3.6)

m_{1}+c_{2}m_{2}+\cdots+c_{j}m_{j}=\gamma\eta.

Substituting for each
m_{i}
we find,

\tau m_{1}+c_{2}\tau m_{2}+\cdots+c_{j}\tau m_{j}=\bigl(\gamma+\beta_{1}+c_{2}\beta_{2}+\cdots+c_{j}\beta_{j}\bigr)\eta,

and since
\partial\eta\in\GL_{\ell}(H)
, it follows that
\gamma+\beta_{1}+c_{2}\beta_{2}+\cdots c_{j}\beta_{j}\in\tau\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{E}}(H)
. Multiplying (
3.3.6
) by
\tau
and subtracting, we have

\bigl(c_{2}^{(1)}-c_{2}\bigr)\tau m_{2}+\cdots+\bigl(c_{j}^{(1)}-c_{j}\bigr)\tau m_{j}=\bigl(\gamma^{(1)}\tau-\gamma-\beta_{1}-c_{2}\beta_{2}-\cdots-c_{j}\beta_{j}\bigr)\eta.

By the previous sentence, the left-hand factor on the right is in
\tau\mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{E}}(H)
, so we can cancel
\tau
from both sides and obtain,

\bigl(c_{2}-c_{2}^{(-1)}\bigr)m_{2}+\cdots+\bigl(c_{j}-c_{j}^{(-1)}\bigr)m_{j}=\bigl(\gamma-\tau^{-1}(\gamma+\beta_{1}+c_{2}\beta_{2}+\cdots+c_{j}\beta_{j})\bigr)\eta.

The minimality of
j
implies
c_{2}=c_{2}^{(-1)},\ldots,c_{j}=c_{j}^{(-1)}
, whence
c_{2},\dots,c_{j}\in H^{\tau}={\mathbb{F}_{q}\langle Z\rangle}
, and thus (
3.3.6
) contradicts the
{\mathbb{F}_{q}\langle Z\rangle}
-linear independence of the classes of
m_{1},\dots,m_{k}
. The desired dimension bound for
(\ker\eta^{*})(H)
then follows from Proposition
2.1.14
(b).

To find the same bound for the
(\ker\eta)(H)
case, we observe that
D^{*}=V^{*}\cdot\eta^{*}\cdot U^{*}
, and so
\deg_{\sigma}d_{i}^{*}=\deg_{\tau}d_{i}
for each
i
. The rest follows exactly as in the
(\ker\eta^{*})(H)
case.
∎

Definition 3.3.7
.

Let
\eta:\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{E}
be given as in Proposition
3.3.4
. We say that
(\ker\eta)(H)
has
full dimension
if its
{\mathbb{F}_{q}\langle Z\rangle}
-dimension is equal to
\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}\deg_{\tau}d_{i}
. Under similar conditions we say
(\ker\eta^{*})(H)
has full dimension.

Corollary 3.3.8
.

Let
\mathcal{E}:\mathcal{A}\to\Mat_{\ell}(H[\tau])
be an abelian and
\mathcal{A}
-finite Anderson
t
-module over
H
with
\rank_{H[t]}\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{E}}(H)=\rank_{H[t]}\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{E}}(H)=r
. For
\nu\in\mathcal{A}
not divisible by the characteristic of
H
, the following hold.

(a)

\mathcal{E}[\nu](H)
has full dimension if and only if
\mathcal{E}[\nu](H)\cong(\mathcal{A}/\nu\mathcal{A})^{r}
as
\mathcal{A}
-modules.

(b)

\mathcal{E}^{*}[\nu](H)
has full dimension if and only if
\mathcal{E}^{*}[\nu](H)\cong(\mathcal{A}/\nu\mathcal{A})^{r}
as
\mathcal{A}
-modules.

Proof.

Combining (
3.3.5
) with the definition of the
\mathcal{A}
-module action on
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{E}}
, we have

\mathcal{E}^{*}[\nu](H)\cong J\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\ker\biggl(\frac{\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{E}}(H)}{\nu\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{E}}(H)}\xrightarrow{(\tau-1)(\cdot)}\frac{\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{E}}(H)}{\nu\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{E}}(H)}\biggr).

As in the proof of Proposition
3.3.4
, any
{\mathbb{F}_{q}\langle Z\rangle}
-basis of
J
is an
H
-linearly independent subset of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{E}}(H)/\nu\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{E}}(H)=\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{E}}(H)/\mathcal{M}_{E}(H)\mathcal{E}_{\nu}
, and is also an
H
-basis since
\mathcal{E}^{*}[\nu]
has full dimension. Thus the natural map

H\otimes_{{\mathbb{F}_{q}\langle Z\rangle}}J\to\frac{\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{E}}(H)}{\nu\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{E}}(H)}

is an isomorphism of
H
-vector spaces and also of
H[t]
-modules. Since
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{E}}(H)/\nu\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{E}}(H)\cong(H[t]/\nu H[t])^{r}
, it follows that
J\cong(\mathcal{A}/\nu\mathcal{A})^{r}
. The argument for
\mathcal{E}[\nu](H)
is similar.
∎

3.4.
Anderson
t
-modules over Tate algebras

We now consider
\widehat{\mathbb{L}}_{z}
as a
\tau
-perfect
\mathbf{A}
-field (taking
{\mathbb{F}_{q}\langle Z\rangle}=\mathbb{F}_{q}(z)
), where
\iota(t)=\theta
, and as noted in §
2.1
,
\tau:\widehat{\mathbb{L}}_{z}\to\widehat{\mathbb{L}}_{z}
is an
\mathbb{F}_{q}(z)
-linear automorphism. We fix an Anderson
t
-module
\mathbf{E}:\mathbf{A}\to\Mat_{\ell}(\widehat{\mathbb{L}}_{z}[\tau])
. Demeslay
[
24
]
*Prop. 2.3
[
25
]
*§1.1.1 observed that it has a unique exponential series

\Exp_{\mathbf{E}}=\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}B_{i}\tau^{i},\quad B_{0}=\mathrm{I}_{\ell},\ B_{i}\in\Mat_{\ell}(\widehat{\mathbb{L}}_{z}),

such that
\Exp_{\mathbf{E}}{}\cdot\partial\mathbf{E}_{a}=\mathbf{E}_{a}\cdot\Exp_{\mathbf{E}}
for
a\in\mathbf{A}
.
Using the arguments of
[
4
]
*Prop. 2.1.4, Demeslay proved that
\lim_{i\to\infty}\deg(B_{i})/q^{i}=-\infty
, and so as in §
2.2.4
the exponential function
\Exp_{\mathbf{E}}:\widehat{\mathbb{L}}_{z}^{\ell}\to\widehat{\mathbb{L}}_{z}^{\ell}
is well-defined on all of
\widehat{\mathbb{L}}_{z}^{\ell}
. If
\Exp_{\mathbf{E}}:\widehat{\mathbb{L}}_{z}^{\ell}\to\widehat{\mathbb{L}}_{z}^{\ell}
is surjective, then
\mathbf{E}
is said to be uniformizable.
Its period lattice is
\Lambda_{\mathbf{E}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\ker\Exp_{\mathbf{E}}\subseteq\widehat{\mathbb{L}}_{z}^{\ell}
.

The logarithm series
\Log_{\mathbf{E}}\in{\Mat_{\ell}(\widehat{\mathbb{L}}_{t})[\![\tau]\!]}
is defined as the inverse of
\Exp_{\mathbf{E}}
,

\Log_{\mathbf{E}}=\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}C_{i}\tau^{i},\quad C_{0}=\mathrm{I}_{\ell},\ C_{i}\in\Mat_{\ell}(\widehat{\mathbb{L}}_{z}),

and satisfies
\partial\mathbf{E}_{a}\cdot\Log_{\mathbf{E}}=\Log_{\mathbf{E}}{}\cdot\mathbf{E}_{a}
for
a\in\mathbf{A}
.
As in the case of constant
t
-modules, the logarithm function
\Log_{\mathbf{E}}(\mathbf{z})
may converge only on an open polydisc in
\widehat{\mathbb{L}}_{z}^{\ell}
.

If
\mathbf{E}
is defined over an
\mathbf{A}
-field
M
with
\mathbb{K}\subseteq M\subseteq\widehat{\mathbb{L}}_{z}
, then
\{B_{i}\}
,
\{C_{i}\}\subseteq\Mat_{\ell}(M)
, even if
\tau:M\to M
is not an automorphism. This follows from the arguments of
[
4
]
*Prop. 2.1.4, Lem. 2.1.6
[
44
]
*Lem. 5.9.3 (see also
[
54
]
*Rem. 2.2.3).

3.4.1.
Discrete subspaces of
\widehat{\mathbb{L}}_{z}
-vector spaces

We adopt the following description of discreteness in
\widehat{\mathbb{L}}_{z}
-vector spaces due to Demeslay
[
8
]
*App. A
[
24
]

[
25
]
. Suppose that
W
is a finite dimensional
\mathbb{K}_{\infty}
-vector space with basis
e_{1},\dots,e_{k}
, and suppose that
\Lambda\subseteq W
is an
\mathbb{F}_{q}(z)
-subspace. Setting
\mathbb{O}_{\infty}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}={\mathbb{F}_{q}(z)[\![\theta^{-1}]\!]}
and
\mathbb{M}_{\infty}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\theta^{-1}{\mathbb{F}_{q}(z)[\![\theta^{-1}]\!]}

to be the valuation ring and maximal ideal of
\mathbb{K}_{\infty}
, the following holds.

Lemma 3.4.2
.

With notation as above, the following are equivalent.

(a)

There exists
n\geqslant 1
such that
\Lambda\cap\bigl(\bigoplus_{i=1}^{k}\mathbb{M}_{\infty}^{n}\cdot e_{i}\bigr)=\{0\}
.

(b)

\Lambda\cap\bigl(\bigoplus_{i=1}^{k}\mathbb{M}_{\infty}\cdot e_{i}\bigr)
is finite dimensional as an
\mathbb{F}_{q}(z)
-vector space.

Proof.

If (b) holds, we note that if
\lambda_{1},\dots,\lambda_{m}
is an
\mathbb{F}_{q}(z)
-basis of
\Lambda\cap\bigl(\bigoplus_{i=1}^{k}\mathbb{M}_{\infty}\cdot e_{i}\bigr)
, then their coordinates in terms of the
\mathbb{K}_{\infty}
-basis
\{e_{i}\}
have bounded
\infty
-adic valuation, which implies (a).
Now suppose that (a) holds and that
\{\lambda_{j}\}
is an infinite
\mathbb{F}_{q}(z)
-linearly independent subset of
\Lambda\cap\bigl(\bigoplus_{i=1}^{k}\mathbb{M}_{\infty}\cdot e_{i}\bigr)
. Since
\mathbb{M}_{\infty}/\mathbb{M}_{\infty}^{n}
is a finite dimensional
\mathbb{F}_{q}(z)
-vector space with basis
\{\theta^{-1},\dots,\theta^{-n+1}\}
, the infinitude of
\{\lambda_{j}\}
implies that some nontrivial
\mathbb{F}_{q}(z)
-linear combination of
\{\lambda_{j}\}
is in
\bigoplus_{i=1}^{k}\mathbb{M}_{\infty}^{n}\cdot e_{i}
. The
\mathbb{F}_{q}(z)
-linear independence of
\{\lambda_{j}\}
then implies that this linear combination is nonzero, which contradicts (a).
∎

Definition 3.4.3
.

An
\mathbb{F}_{q}(z)
-subspace
\Lambda\subseteq W
is
discrete
if the equivalent conditions of Lemma
3.4.2
hold. We note that this is independent of the choice of
\mathbb{K}_{\infty}
-basis of
W
. Furthermore, if
V
is instead a finite dimensional
\widehat{\mathbb{L}}_{z}
-vector space and
\Lambda\subseteq V
is an
\mathbb{F}_{q}(z)
-subspace, then we say
\Lambda
is
discrete in
V
if it is discrete in
W=\Span_{\mathbb{K}_{\infty}}(\Lambda)
.

Lemma 3.4.4

(Demeslay
[
24
]
*Lem. 2.1
[
25
]
*Lem. 2.1.2)
.

Let
\Lambda
be an
\mathbb{A}
-submodule of a
\mathbb{K}_{\infty}
-vector space
W
of dimension
k\geqslant 1
. The following are equivalent.

(a)

\Lambda
is a free
\mathbb{A}
-module of rank
k
, and
\Span_{\mathbb{K}_{\infty}}(\Lambda)=W
.

(b)

\Lambda
is a discrete
\mathbb{F}_{q}(z)
-subspace of
W
, and every open subspace of the
\mathbb{F}_{q}(z)
-vector space
W/\Lambda
has finite codimension.

Definition 3.4.5
.

An
\mathbb{A}
-submodule
\Lambda
of a
k
-dimensional
\mathbb{K}_{\infty}
-vector space
W
is called an
\mathbb{A}
-lattice
if it satisfies the equivalent conditions of Lemma
3.4.4
. More generally, if
\Lambda
is an
\mathbb{A}
-submodule of a finite dimensional
\widehat{\mathbb{L}}_{z}
-vector space
V
, then
\Lambda
is an
\mathbb{A}
-lattice in
V
if it is an
\mathbb{A}
-lattice in
W=\Span_{\mathbb{K}_{\infty}}(\Lambda)
.

3.4.6.
Exponential functions and period lattices

Let
\mathbf{E}:\mathbf{A}\to\Mat_{\ell}(\widehat{\mathbb{L}}_{z}[\tau])
be an Anderson
t
-module. Its exponential function
\Exp_{\mathbf{E}}:\widehat{\mathbb{L}}_{z}^{\ell}\to\widehat{\mathbb{L}}_{z}^{\ell}
is locally an isometry
[
24
]
*§2.2 (cf.
[
33
]
*Lem. 3.3.2
[
47
]
*Lem. 2.5.4). Therefore, we can find
\epsilon>0
such that on the open polydisc,
\mathrm{D}=\{\mathbf{z}\in\widehat{\mathbb{L}}_{z}^{\ell}\mid\lVert\mathbf{z}\rVert<\epsilon\}
, we have

(3.4.7)

\mathbf{z}\in\mathrm{D}\quad\Rightarrow\quad\lVert\Exp_{\mathbf{E}}(\mathbf{z})\rVert=\lVert\mathbf{z}\rVert.

Moreover,
\Log_{\mathbf{E}}(\mathbf{z})
converges on
\mathrm{D}
. We thus have following lemma.

Lemma 3.4.8
.

For an Anderson
t
-module
\mathbf{E}:\mathbf{A}\to\Mat_{\ell}(\widehat{\mathbb{L}}_{z}[\tau])
, let
\Lambda_{\mathbf{E}}=\ker\Exp_{\mathbf{E}}
. The following hold.

(a)

\Lambda_{\mathbf{E}}
is a discrete
\mathbb{F}_{q}(z)
-subspace of
\widehat{\mathbb{L}}_{z}^{\ell}
.

(b)

If
\mathbf{E}
is a Drinfeld module over
\widehat{\mathbb{L}}_{z}
, then
\Lambda_{\mathbf{E}}\subseteq\widehat{\mathbb{L}}_{z}
is an
\mathbb{A}
-lattice.

Proof.

Let
W=\Span_{\mathbb{K}_{\infty}}(\Lambda_{\mathbf{E}})
. Choosing
\mathrm{D}
as in (
3.4.7
), it must be that
\Lambda_{\mathbf{E}}\cap\mathrm{D}=\{0\}
. It follows that
\Lambda_{\mathbf{E}}
is discrete by Lemma
3.4.2
. If
\mathbf{E}
is a Drinfeld module, then
\Lambda\subseteq W
is a discrete
\mathbb{A}
-submodule. Letting
\lambda_{1},\dots,\lambda_{k}\in\Lambda_{\mathbf{E}}
be a
\mathbb{K}_{\infty}
-basis of
W
and setting
\Lambda^{\prime}=\Span_{\mathbb{A}}(\lambda_{1},\dots,\lambda_{k})
, we see that
W/\Lambda^{\prime}\cong\bigoplus_{i=1}^{k}\mathbb{M}_{\infty}\cdot\lambda_{i}
. Since
\Lambda_{\mathbf{E}}\subseteq W
is discrete, Lemma
3.4.2
(b) implies that
\Lambda\cap\bigl(\bigoplus_{i=1}^{k}\mathbb{M}_{\infty}\cdot\lambda_{i}\bigr)
is a finite dimensional
\mathbb{F}_{q}(z)
-vector space, and so
\Lambda_{\mathbf{E}}/\Lambda^{\prime}
itself has finite dimension over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(z)
. Therefore,
\Lambda_{\mathbf{E}}
is finitely generated and free as an
\mathbb{A}
-module with rank
k
. Thus
\Lambda_{\mathbf{E}}
is an
\mathbb{A}
-lattice by Lemma
3.4.4
.
∎

We should note that Lemma
3.4.8
(b) does not necessarily imply that when
\mathbf{E}
is a Drinfeld module of rank
r
over
\widehat{\mathbb{L}}_{z}
, that its period lattice has rank
r
as an
\mathbb{A}
-module. Nevertheless, for constant Drinfeld modules over
\widehat{\mathbb{L}}_{z}
this is indeed the case.

Theorem 3.4.9

(Gezmiş-Papanikolas
[
33
]
*Thm. 7.1.1, Prop. 8.2.1)
.

Let
\phi:\mathbb{A}\to\mathbb{C}_{\infty}[\tau]
be a Drinfeld module of rank
r
. As a Drinfeld module over
\widehat{\mathbb{L}}_{z}
, the following hold.

(a)

\Exp_{\phi}:\widehat{\mathbb{L}}_{z}\to\widehat{\mathbb{L}}_{z}
is surjective.

(b)

\Lambda_{\phi}=\ker\Exp_{\phi}\subseteq\widehat{\mathbb{L}}_{z}
is free of rank
r
over
\mathbb{A}
.

(c)

If
\pi_{1},\dots,\pi_{r}\in\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
form an
A
-basis of
\ker(\Exp_{\phi}|_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}})
, then

\Lambda_{\phi}=\mathbb{A}\pi_{1}+\cdots+\mathbb{A}\pi_{r}
.

Remark 3.4.10
.

In
[
33
]
, the Drinfeld modules under consideration are Drinfeld modules over
\mathbb{T}_{z}
, or more generally over Tate algebras of several variables. However, the arguments needed for Theorem
3.4.9
, originally due to Anderson for constant
t
-modules, transfer equally well to Drinfeld modules over
\widehat{\mathbb{L}}_{z}
with little modification. The primary required information is that
\widehat{\mathbb{L}}_{z}
is complete, that
\Exp_{\phi}:\widehat{\mathbb{L}}_{z}\to\widehat{\mathbb{L}}_{z}
is locally an isometry, and that
\Lambda_{\phi}\subseteq\widehat{\mathbb{L}}_{z}
is discrete. As there is little change from
[
33
]
we do not include the details. See also
[
47
]
*§2.5
[
54
]
*§3.4 for more information concerning the case of
t
-modules.

3.5.
t
-modules in finite characteristic

We now consider a more specific situation. Fix
f\in A_{+}
irreducible of degree
d
, and let
\mathbb{F}_{f}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=A/fA
. We make
\mathbb{F}_{f}
into an
\mathsf{A}
-field by by picking a root
\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5mu\theta}\in\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
of
f(t)=0
and defining
\iota:\mathsf{A}\to\mathbb{F}_{f}
by
t\mapsto\mkern 2.5mu\overline{\mkern-2.5mu\theta}
. As in §
3.1
, we let
Z=\{z_{1},\dots,z_{n}\}
for
n\geqslant 0
, thus defining fields,
{\mathbb{F}_{q}\langle Z\rangle}\subseteq{\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}\subseteq{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}
. We note that

(3.5.1)

\Gal({\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}/{\mathbb{F}_{q}\langle Z\rangle})\cong\Gal(\mathbb{F}_{f}/\mathbb{F}_{q}),

and that

(3.5.2)

\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}(Z)/\mathbb{F}_{f}(Z))\cong\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}/\mathbb{F}_{f}),\quad\Aut({\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}(\!(Z)\!)}/{\mathbb{F}_{f}(\!(Z)\!)})\hookleftarrow\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}/\mathbb{F}_{f}).

We identify these Galois groups going forward, and in particular, when we refer to the “Galois action” or “Galois equivariance,” it will be through the groups
\Gal(\mathbb{F}_{f}/\mathbb{F}_{q})
or
\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}/\mathbb{F}_{f})
identified as either
\Gal({\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}/{\mathbb{F}_{q}\langle Z\rangle})
or (a subgroup of)
\Aut({\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}/{\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle})
.
As in §
3.1
, the inclusion
\mathbb{F}_{f}\subseteq{\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}
extends
\iota
,

\iota:\mathcal{A}\to{\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}\subseteq{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}\langle Z\rangle},

to make
{\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}
and
{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}
into
\tau
-perfect
\mathcal{A}
-fields. Moreover,
\tau
is the generator of
\Gal({\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}/{\mathbb{F}_{q}\langle Z\rangle})
. Also
\tau^{d}
generates
\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}/\mathbb{F}_{f})
, and in the two cases in (
3.5.2
) we have
\smash{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}(Z)}^{\tau^{d}}=\mathbb{F}_{f}(Z)
and
\smash{{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}(\!(Z)\!)}}^{\tau^{d}}={\mathbb{F}_{f}(\!(Z)\!)}
, so in both cases
\smash{{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}}^{\tau^{d}}={\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}
.

We now fix an abelian and
\mathcal{A}
-finite
t
-module
\mathcal{E}:\mathcal{A}\to\Mat_{\ell}({\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}[\tau])
, such that the ranks of its
t
-motive and dual
t
-motive agree, and we let
\mathcal{E}^{*}:\mathcal{A}\to\Mat_{\ell}({\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}[\sigma])
be its adjoint. Because
\mathcal{E}
is defined over
{\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}
, we see that
\tau^{d}\cdot\mathrm{I}_{\ell}:\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{E}
and
\sigma^{d}\cdot\mathrm{I}_{\ell}:\mathcal{E}^{*}\to\mathcal{E}^{*}
are endomorphisms. Fix also
{\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}[t]
-bases,

\mathbf{m}=(m_{1},\dots,m_{r})^{{\mathsf{T}}}\in\Mat_{r\times 1}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{E}}({\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle})),\quad\mathbf{n}=(n_{1},\dots,n_{r})^{{\mathsf{T}}}\in\Mat_{r\times 1}(\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{E}}({\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle})),

as in §
3.2.7
, as well as
\Gamma
,
\Phi\in\Mat_{r}({\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}[t])
, so that

\tau\mathbf{m}=\Gamma\mathbf{m}
,
\sigma\mathbf{n}=\Phi\mathbf{n}
.
We note that for each
i
, we have
m_{i}\in\Mat_{1\times\ell}({\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}[\tau])
and
n_{i}\in\Mat_{1\times\ell}({\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}[\sigma])
. Consider the
{\mathbb{F}_{q}\langle Z\rangle}[t]
-linear isomorphisms

\mathbf{i}:\Mat_{1\times r}({\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}[t])\to\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{E}}({\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}),\quad\mathbf{j}:\Mat_{1\times r}({\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}[t])\to\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{E}}({\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}),

given by

\displaystyle\mathbf{i}(\mathbf{u})

\displaystyle=\mathbf{i}(u_{1},\dots,u_{r})=\mathbf{u}\cdot\mathbf{m}=u_{1}m_{1}+\cdots+u_{r}m_{r},

\displaystyle\mathbf{j}(\mathbf{u})

\displaystyle=\mathbf{j}(u_{1},\dots,u_{r})=\mathbf{u}\cdot\mathbf{n}=u_{1}n_{1}+\cdots+u_{r}n_{r}.

The maps
\mathbf{i}
and
\mathbf{j}
are Anderson
t
-frames in the sense of
[
19
]
*§2.3.2
[
33
]
*§4.4
[
54
]
*§3.2. Finally, we let

(3.5.3)

\mathrm{G}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\Gamma^{(d-1)}\cdots\Gamma^{(1)}\Gamma,\quad\mathrm{H}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\Phi^{(-d+1)}\cdots\Phi^{(-1)}\Phi,

and then we represent
\tau^{d}
and
\sigma^{d}
in terms of our
{\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}[t]
-bases.

Lemma 3.5.4
.

The following diagrams of
{\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}[t]
-modules commute:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Mat_{1\times r}({\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}[t])}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{E}}({\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}\langle Z\rangle})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Mat_{1\times r}({\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}[t])}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{E}}({\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}\langle Z\rangle})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Mat_{1\times r}({\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}[t])}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{E}}({\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}\langle Z\rangle})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Mat_{1\times r}({\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}[t])}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{E}}({\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\cdot)\mathrm{G}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\cdot)\tau^{d}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{j}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\cdot)\mathrm{H}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\cdot)\sigma^{d}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{j}}

Proof.

We first note that since the entries of
\mathbf{m}
are in
\Mat_{1\times\ell}({\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}[\tau])
, we have
\tau^{d}\mathbf{m}=\mathbf{m}\tau^{d}
. Furthermore, we have
\tau^{d}\mathbf{m}=\Gamma^{(d-1)}\cdots\Gamma^{(1)}\Gamma\mathbf{m}=\mathrm{G}\mathbf{m}
, and so
\mathrm{G}\mathbf{m}=\mathbf{m}\tau^{d}
. For
\mathbf{u}\in\Mat_{1\times r}({\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}[t])
, we thus have
\mathbf{i}(\mathbf{u})\tau^{d}=\mathbf{i}(\mathbf{u}\mathrm{G})
, and the first diagram commutes. The second diagram is the same.
∎

Taking
\eta=(1-\tau^{d})\cdot\mathrm{I}_{\ell}
as an endomorphism of
\mathcal{E}({\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}\langle Z\rangle})
, (
3.3.2
) and (
3.3.3
) together with Lemma
3.5.4
imply the following result. We note that the kernels of
(1-\tau^{d})\mathrm{I}_{\ell}
and
(1-\sigma^{d})\mathrm{I}_{\ell}
have full dimension in the sense of Definition
3.3.7
.

Lemma 3.5.5
.

Let
\mathcal{E}:\mathcal{A}\to\Mat_{\ell}({\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}[\tau])
be an abelian and
\mathcal{A}
-finite
t
-module of rank
r
. With
\mathrm{G}
,
\mathrm{H}\in\GL_{r}({\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}[t])
as above, we have
\mathcal{A}
-module isomorphisms,

(a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{*}({\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle})\cong\ker\biggl(\frac{\Mat_{1\times r}({\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}[t])}{\Mat_{1\times r}({\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}[t])(\mathrm{I}-\mathrm{G})}\xrightarrow{(1-\tau)(\cdot)}\frac{\Mat_{1\times r}({\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}[t])}{\Mat_{1\times r}({\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}[t])(\mathrm{I}-\mathrm{G})}\biggr)
,

(b)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}({\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle})\cong\ker\biggl(\frac{\Mat_{1\times r}({\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}[t])}{\Mat_{1\times r}({\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}[t])(\mathrm{I}-\mathrm{H})}\xrightarrow{(1-\sigma)(\cdot)}\frac{\Mat_{1\times r}({\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}[t])}{\Mat_{1\times r}({\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}[t])(\mathrm{I}-\mathrm{H})}\biggr)
,

where
\mathrm{I}=\mathrm{I}_{r}
.

3.6.
Poonen pairings

We now investigate generalizations of pairings defined by Poonen
[
63
]
for Drinfeld modules over finite fields to
t
-modules over
{\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}
. For additional exposition in the case of Drinfeld modules see
[
44
]
*§4.14
[
71
]
*§2.10.

We fix a
t
-module
\mathcal{E}:\mathcal{A}\to\Mat_{\ell}({\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}[\tau])
, determined by

(3.6.1)

\mathcal{E}_{t}=\partial\mathcal{E}_{t}+E_{1}\tau+\cdots+E_{w}\tau^{w},\quad E_{i}\in\Mat_{\ell}({\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}).

We also fix an endomorphism
\eta:\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{E}
over
{\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}
with

(3.6.2)

\eta=N_{0}+N_{1}\tau^{1}+\cdots+N_{m}\tau^{m},\quad N_{i}\in\Mat_{\ell}({\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}),\ \det N_{0}\neq 0.

We let

\ker\eta\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\{\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{E}({\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}\langle Z\rangle})\mid\eta(\mathbf{x})=0\},\quad\ker\eta^{*}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\{\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{E}^{*}({\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}\langle Z\rangle})\mid\eta^{*}(\mathbf{x})=0\},

and we note from Proposition
3.3.4
that these are finite dimensional
{\mathbb{F}_{q}\langle Z\rangle}
-vector spaces. We say that
\ker\eta
and
\ker\eta^{*}
have full dimension if they have full dimension in the sense of Definition
3.3.7
while taking
H={\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}
. Furthermore, if
\eta=\mathcal{E}_{a}
for
a\in\mathcal{A}
, we write
\mathcal{E}[a]\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=(\ker\mathcal{E}_{a})({\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}\langle Z\rangle})
and
\mathcal{E}^{*}[a]\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=(\ker\mathcal{E}_{a}^{*})({\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}\langle Z\rangle})
.

We now follow the exposition in
[
44
]
*§4.14
[
63
]
*§9 to construct a pairing between
\ker\eta
and
\ker\eta^{*}
. Letting
\mathbf{x}\in\ker\eta
and
\mathbf{y}\in\ker\eta^{*}
, we see from (
3.2.4
) and (
3.2.5
) that

\eta(\mathbf{x})=0\quad\Rightarrow\quad\delta_{1}\bigl(\mathbf{x}^{{\mathsf{T}}}\eta^{*}\bigr)=0,\quad\eta^{*}(\mathbf{y})=0\quad\Rightarrow\quad\gamma_{1}\bigl(\mathbf{y}^{{\mathsf{T}}}\eta\bigr)=0.

Therefore, (
3.2.4
) and (
3.2.5
) also imply that there are unique
g_{\mathbf{x}}\in\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{E}}({\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}\langle Z\rangle})=\Mat_{1\times\ell}({\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}[\sigma])
and
h_{\mathbf{y}}\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{E}}({\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}\langle Z\rangle})=\Mat_{1\times\ell}({\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}[\tau])
so that

(3.6.3)

\mathbf{x}^{{\mathsf{T}}}\eta^{*}=(1-\sigma)g_{\mathbf{x}},\quad\mathbf{y}^{{\mathsf{T}}}\eta=(1-\tau)h_{\mathbf{y}}.

We then define

(3.6.4)

\langle\mathbf{x}\mathbin{,}\mathbf{y}\rangle_{\eta}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=g_{\mathbf{x}}(\mathbf{y})=(g_{\mathbf{x}}\cdot\mathbf{y})(1),

where
g_{\mathbf{x}}(\mathbf{y})
is the
\sigma
-analogue of the evaluation in (
2.1.7
) and
(g_{\mathbf{x}}\cdot\mathbf{y})(1)
is the product of twisted polynomials in
\sigma
evaluated at
1
.
This defines a pairing on
{\ker\eta}\times{\ker\eta^{*}}
with the following properties.

Proposition 3.6.5

(cf. Poonen
[
44
]
*pp. 126–129
[
63
]
*§8)
.

The pairing defined above satisfies

\langle\cdot\mathbin{,}\cdot\rangle_{\eta}:{\ker\eta}\times{\ker\eta^{*}}\to{\mathbb{F}_{q}\langle Z\rangle},

and it is
{\mathbb{F}_{q}\langle Z\rangle}
-bilinear. Furthermore, the following hold.

(a)

For
\mathbf{x}\in\ker\eta
and
\mathbf{y}\in\ker\eta^{*}
, we also have
\langle\mathbf{x}\mathbin{,}\mathbf{y}\rangle_{\eta}=h_{\mathbf{y}}(\mathbf{x})
.

(b)

\langle\cdot\mathbin{,}\cdot\rangle_{\eta}
is
\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}/\mathbb{F}_{f})
-equivariant.

(c)

If
\ker\eta
and
\ker\eta^{*}
have full dimension, then
\langle\cdot\mathbin{,}\cdot\rangle_{\eta}
is non-degenerate.

Proof.

Taking
\mathbf{x}^{{\mathsf{T}}}\eta^{*}=(1-\sigma)g_{\mathbf{x}}
from (
3.6.3
) and evaluating at
\mathbf{y}
, we have

\bigl(\mathbf{x}^{{\mathsf{T}}}\eta^{*}\bigr)(\mathbf{y})=\mathbf{x}^{{\mathsf{T}}}(\eta^{*}(\mathbf{y}))=0=\big((1-\sigma)g_{\mathbf{x}}\bigr)(\mathbf{y})=(1-\sigma)(g_{\mathbf{x}}(\mathbf{y})),

where we have used the associativity of (
2.1.10
). But
(1-\sigma)(g_{\mathbf{x}}(\mathbf{y}))=g_{\mathbf{x}}(\mathbf{y})-\sigma(g_{\mathbf{x}}(\mathbf{y}))
, and so
g_{\mathbf{x}}(\mathbf{y})\in{\mathbb{F}_{q}\langle Z\rangle}
. It is straightforward to check that
\langle\cdot\mathbin{,}\cdot\rangle_{\eta}
is
{\mathbb{F}_{q}\langle Z\rangle}
-bilinear.

We next verify (a) (cf.
[
44
]
*Prop. 4.14.10
[
63
]
*Prop. 14). By (
3.6.4
), we need to show

(g_{\mathbf{x}}\cdot\mathbf{y})(1)=(h_{\mathbf{y}}\cdot\mathbf{x})(1).

From (
3.6.3
) we have the equality of polynomials in
{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}[\tau]
,

(3.6.6)

\mathbf{y}^{{\mathsf{T}}}\cdot g_{\mathbf{x}}^{*}(1-\tau)=\mathbf{y}^{{\mathsf{T}}}\cdot\eta\cdot\mathbf{x}=(1-\tau)h_{\mathbf{y}}\cdot\mathbf{x}.

First, since
(\mathbf{y}^{{\mathsf{T}}}g_{\mathbf{x}}^{*}(1-\tau))(1)=0
, it follows that
(1-\tau)(h_{\mathbf{y}}(\mathbf{x})(1))=0
and so
(h_{\mathbf{y}}\cdot\mathbf{x})(1)\in{\mathbb{F}_{q}\langle Z\rangle}
. Second, this shows that
\mathbf{y}^{{\mathsf{T}}}\cdot g_{\mathbf{x}}^{*}
and
h_{\mathbf{y}}\cdot\mathbf{x}
have the same degree in
\tau
. If

\mathbf{y}^{{\mathsf{T}}}\cdot g_{\mathbf{x}}^{*}=\sum_{i=0}^{n}b_{i}\tau^{i},\quad h_{\mathbf{y}}\cdot\mathbf{x}=\sum_{i=0}^{n}c_{i}\tau^{i},\quad b_{i},\ c_{i}\in{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}\langle Z\rangle},

then we need to show

(g_{\mathbf{x}}\cdot\mathbf{y})(1)=b_{0}+b_{1}^{(-1)}+\dots+b_{n}^{(-n)}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle?}}{{=}}c_{0}+c_{1}+\dots+c_{n}=(h_{\mathbf{y}}\cdot\mathbf{x})(1).

Multiplying (
3.6.6
) on both sides by
(1-\tau)^{-1}
in
{{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}[\![\tau]\!]}
, we have

(1+\tau+\tau^{2}+\cdots)\cdot\mathbf{y}^{{\mathsf{T}}}\cdot g_{\mathbf{x}}^{*}=h_{\mathbf{y}}\cdot\mathbf{x}\cdot(1+\tau+\tau^{2}+\cdots)\quad\in{{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}[\![\tau]\!]}.

Comparing coefficients of
\tau^{n}
on both sides, we obtain

b_{0}^{(n)}+b_{1}^{(n-1)}+\cdots+b_{n}=c_{0}+c_{1}+\cdots+c_{n}=h_{\mathbf{y}}(\mathbf{x}).

Since
h_{\mathbf{y}}(\mathbf{x})\in{\mathbb{F}_{q}\langle Z\rangle}
, it follows that
h_{\mathbf{y}}(\mathbf{x})=\sigma^{n}(b_{0}^{(n)}+b_{1}^{(n-1)}+\cdots+b_{n})=b_{0}+b_{1}^{(-1)}+\cdots+b_{n}^{(-n)}
, and the desired result follows.

To verify Galois equivariance, since
\eta
is defined over
{\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}
, we find from (
3.6.3
) that

\tau^{d}\mathbf{y}^{{\mathsf{T}}}\eta=\bigl(\mathbf{y}^{(d)}\bigr)^{{\mathsf{T}}}\eta\tau^{d}=(1-\tau)\tau^{d}h_{\mathbf{y}}=h_{\mathbf{y}}^{(d)}\tau^{d}\in\Mat_{1\times\ell}({\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}[\tau]).

Thus if
\alpha\in\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}/\mathbb{F}_{f})
is the
q^{d}
-th power Frobenius automorphism,
h_{\alpha(\mathbf{y})}=h_{\mathbf{y}}^{(d)}
. Therefore, from (a) we have

\langle\alpha(\mathbf{x})\mathbin{,}\alpha(\mathbf{y})\rangle_{\eta}=h_{\alpha(\mathbf{y})}(\alpha(\mathbf{x}))=h_{\mathbf{y}}^{(d)}\bigl(\mathbf{x}^{(d)}\bigr)=\alpha(h_{\mathbf{y}}(\mathbf{x}))=\alpha(\langle\mathbf{x}\mathbin{,}\mathbf{y}\rangle_{\eta})=\langle\mathbf{x}\mathbin{,}\mathbf{y}\rangle_{\eta},

which proves (b).

For (c), we assume
(\ker\eta)({\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}\langle Z\rangle})
and
(\ker\eta^{*})({\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}\langle Z\rangle})
have full dimension as in Definition
3.3.7
. After multiplying
\eta
on the right and left by invertible matrices from
\GL_{\ell}({\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}[\tau])
as in Proposition
2.1.14
, we can assume that
\eta=\diag(\eta_{1},\dots,\eta_{\ell})\in\Mat_{\ell}({\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}[\tau])
. By the definition of full dimension, Proposition
3.3.4
implies that

\dim_{{\mathbb{F}_{q}\langle Z\rangle}}\ker\eta=\dim_{{\mathbb{F}_{q}\langle Z\rangle}}\ker\eta^{*}=\deg_{\tau}\eta_{1}+\cdots+\deg_{\tau}\eta_{\ell}.

Furthermore, we have the canonical isomorphisms of
{\mathbb{F}_{q}\langle Z\rangle}
-vector spaces,

\ker\eta\cong\ker\eta_{1}\oplus\cdots\oplus\ker\eta_{\ell},\quad\ker\eta^{*}\cong\ker\eta_{1}^{*}\oplus\cdots\oplus\ker\eta_{\ell}^{*},

with
\dim_{{\mathbb{F}_{q}\langle Z\rangle}}\ker\eta_{i}=\dim_{{\mathbb{F}_{q}\langle Z\rangle}}\ker\eta_{i}^{*}=\deg_{\tau}\eta_{i}
for each
i
.

Now suppose that
\mathbf{x}\in\ker\eta
satisfies that
\langle\mathbf{x}\mathbin{,}\mathbf{y}\rangle_{\eta}=0
for all
\mathbf{y}\in\ker\eta^{*}
. Write
\mathbf{x}=(x_{1},\dots,x_{\ell})^{{\mathsf{T}}}
for
x_{1},\dots,x_{\ell}\in{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}
. If we write
g_{\mathbf{x}}=(g_{1},\dots,g_{\ell})\in\Mat_{1\times\ell}({\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}[\sigma])
, then (
3.6.3
) implies

x_{i}\eta_{i}^{*}=(1-\sigma)g_{i},\quad\forall i,\ 1\leqslant i\leqslant\ell.

Suppose we have
j
with
x_{j}\neq 0
. Then
\deg_{\sigma}g_{j}=\deg_{\sigma}\eta_{j}^{*}-1
. But for all
y_{j}\in\ker\eta_{j}^{*}
, if we let
\mathbf{y}=(0,\dots,0,y_{j},0,\dots 0)^{{\mathsf{T}}}\in\ker\eta^{*}
, where
y_{j}
is in the
j
-th entry, then

\langle\mathbf{x}\mathbin{,}\mathbf{y}\rangle_{\eta}=g_{j}(y_{j})=0.

Since
\ker\eta_{j}^{*}
has dimension
\deg_{\sigma}\eta_{j}^{*}
over
{\mathbb{F}_{q}\langle Z\rangle}
, we see that
\ker g_{j}
has larger
{\mathbb{F}_{q}\langle Z\rangle}
-dimension than its degree in
\sigma
would allow by Lemma
3.3.1
. Thus it must be that
\mathbf{x}=0
. Similarly the kernel on the right of
\langle\cdot\mathbin{,}\cdot\rangle_{\eta}
is trivial.
∎

3.6.7.
The case
\eta=(1-\tau^{d})\mathrm{I}_{\ell}

Let
\mathcal{E}:\mathcal{A}\to\Mat_{\ell}({\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}[\tau])
be a
t
-module over
{\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}
. Then
\eta=(1-\tau^{d})\mathrm{I}_{\ell}
is an endomorphism of
\mathcal{E}
, and we have
\ker\eta=\mathcal{E}({\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle})={\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}^{\ell}
and
\ker\eta^{*}=\mathcal{E}^{*}({\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle})={\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}^{\ell}
. As
[\mathbb{F}_{f}:\mathbb{F}_{q}]=d
, we see that
\ker\eta
and
\ker\eta^{*}
have full dimension. For
\mathbf{x}\in{\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}^{\ell}
,

\mathbf{x}^{{\mathsf{T}}}\eta^{*}=(1-\sigma^{d})\mathbf{x}^{{\mathsf{T}}}=(1-\sigma)\Bigl(\mathbf{x}^{{\mathsf{T}}}+\bigl(\mathbf{x}^{(-1)}\bigr)^{{\mathsf{T}}}\sigma+\cdots+\bigl(\mathbf{x}^{(-d+1)}\bigr)^{{\mathsf{T}}}\sigma^{d-1}\Bigr).

Thus by (
3.6.3
), we have
g_{\mathbf{x}}(\mathbf{y})=\mathbf{x}^{{\mathsf{T}}}\mathbf{y}+(\mathbf{x}^{(-1)})^{{\mathsf{T}}}\mathbf{y}^{(-1)}+\cdots+(\mathbf{x}^{(-d+1)})^{{\mathsf{T}}}\mathbf{y}^{(-d+1)}
for
\mathbf{y}\in{\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}^{\ell}
. From this we see that

(3.6.8)

\langle\mathbf{x}\mathbin{,}\mathbf{y}\rangle_{\eta}=\Tr^{\mathbb{F}_{f}}_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}\bigl(\mathbf{x}^{{\mathsf{T}}}\mathbf{y}\bigr),

and so in this case

\langle\cdot\mathbin{,}\cdot\rangle_{\eta}:\mathcal{E}({\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle})\times\mathcal{E}^{*}({\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle})\to{\mathbb{F}_{q}\langle Z\rangle}

coincides with the trace pairing from
{\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}
to
{\mathbb{F}_{q}\langle Z\rangle}
. This could be predicted by
[
44
]
*Prop. 4.14.11
[
63
]
*Prop. 18, which was inspired by work of Elkies.

Returning to the general case of an endomorphism
\eta:\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{E}
with
\det(\partial\eta)\neq 0
, we establish an adjoint relationship between
\mathcal{E}_{a}
and
\langle\cdot\mathbin{,}\cdot\rangle_{\eta}
.

Proposition 3.6.9

(cf.
[
44
]
*Prop. 4.14.13
[
63
]
*Prop. 19)
.

Let
\mathcal{E}:\mathcal{A}\to\Mat_{\ell}({\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}[\tau])
be a
t
-module over
{\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}
, and let
\eta:\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{E}
be an endomorphism. Then for any
a\in\mathcal{A}
,

\langle\mathcal{E}_{a}(\mathbf{x})\mathbin{,}\mathbf{y}\rangle_{\eta}=\langle\mathbf{x}\mathbin{,}\mathcal{E}_{a}^{*}(\mathbf{y})\rangle_{\eta},\quad\forall\,\mathbf{x}\in\ker\eta,\ \mathbf{y}\in\ker\eta^{*}.

Proof.

We pick
g_{\mathbf{x}}
and
g_{\mathcal{E}_{a}(\mathbf{x})}
as in (
3.6.3
). Then

\mathbf{x}^{{\mathsf{T}}}\eta^{*}\mathcal{E}_{a}^{*}=(1-\sigma)g_{\mathbf{x}}\mathcal{E}_{a}^{*},\quad\mathcal{E}_{a}(\mathbf{x})^{{\mathsf{T}}}\eta^{*}=(1-\sigma)g_{\mathcal{E}_{a}(\mathbf{x})},

and after subtracting these equations and noting that
\eta^{*}\mathcal{E}_{a}^{*}=\mathcal{E}_{a}^{*}\eta^{*}
, we obtain

\bigl(\mathbf{x}^{{\mathsf{T}}}\mathcal{E}_{a}^{*}-\mathcal{E}_{a}(\mathbf{x})^{{\mathsf{T}}}\bigr)\eta^{*}=(1-\sigma)\bigl(g_{\mathbf{x}}\mathcal{E}_{a}^{*}-g_{\mathcal{E}_{a}(\mathbf{x})}\bigr).

Since
\delta_{1}(\mathbf{x}^{{\mathsf{T}}}\mathcal{E}_{a}^{*}-\mathcal{E}_{a}(\mathbf{x})^{{\mathsf{T}}})=0
, (
3.2.5
) implies that
\mathbf{x}^{{\mathsf{T}}}\mathcal{E}_{a}^{*}-\mathcal{E}_{a}(\mathbf{x})^{{\mathsf{T}}}=(1-\sigma)\mathbf{h}
for some
\mathbf{h}\in\Mat_{1\times\ell}({\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}[\sigma])
. Therefore,
\mathbf{h}\cdot\eta^{*}=g_{\mathbf{x}}\mathcal{E}_{a}^{*}-g_{\mathcal{E}_{a}(\mathbf{x})}
.
Evaluating at
\mathbf{y}\in\ker\eta^{*}
,

0=\mathbf{h}\bigl(\eta^{*}(\mathbf{y})\bigr)=g_{\mathbf{x}}\bigl(\mathcal{E}_{a}^{*}(\mathbf{y})\bigr)-g_{\mathcal{E}_{a}(\mathbf{x})}(\mathbf{y})=\langle\mathbf{x}\mathbin{,}\mathcal{E}_{a}^{*}(\mathbf{y})\rangle_{\eta}-\langle\mathcal{E}_{a}(\mathbf{x})\mathbin{,}\mathbf{y}\rangle_{\eta}.\qed

As in Poonen
[
63
]
*§9, we can use Proposition
3.6.9
to define an
\mathcal{A}
-bilinear pairing as follows. Let
\mathcal{A}^{\wedge}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\Hom_{{\mathbb{F}_{q}\langle Z\rangle}}(\mathcal{A},{\mathbb{F}_{q}\langle Z\rangle})
, which carries an
\mathcal{A}
-module structure in the usual fashion. Then for an endomorphism
\eta:\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{E}
, we define

(3.6.10)

[\cdot\mathbin{,}\cdot]_{\eta}:\ker\eta\times\ker\eta^{*}\to\mathcal{A}^{\wedge}

by

[\mathbf{x}\mathbin{,}\mathbf{y}]_{\eta}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\bigl(a\mapsto\langle\mathcal{E}_{a}(\mathbf{x})\mathbin{,}\mathbf{y}\rangle_{\eta}\bigr).

As shown earlier in the section, since
\det(\partial\eta)\neq 0
, the
{\mathbb{F}_{q}\langle Z\rangle}
-vector spaces
\ker\eta
and
\ker\eta^{*}
are finite dimensional, and thus they are finitely generated and torsion
\mathcal{A}
-modules. Letting
h={[\ker\eta]}_{\mathcal{A}}
, it follows that
[\cdot\mathbin{,}\cdot]_{\eta}
takes values in
(\mathcal{A}/h\mathcal{A})^{\wedge}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\Hom_{{\mathbb{F}_{q}\langle Z\rangle}}(\mathcal{A}/h\mathcal{A},{\mathbb{F}_{q}\langle Z\rangle})

\subseteq\mathcal{A}^{\wedge}
. The former is a finitely generated torsion
\mathcal{A}
-module isomorphic to
\mathcal{A}/h\mathcal{A}
.

Proposition 3.6.11

(cf.
[
44
]
*Prop. 4.14.14
[
63
]
*Prop. 19)
.

Let
\eta:\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{E}
be an endomorphism over
{\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}
with
\det(\partial\eta)\neq 0
, and let
h={[\ker\eta]}_{\mathcal{A}}
. Define the pairing

[\cdot\mathbin{,}\cdot]_{\eta}:\ker\eta\times\ker\eta^{*}\to(\mathcal{A}/h\mathcal{A})^{\wedge}

as in (
3.6.10
).

(a)

[\cdot\mathbin{,}\cdot]_{\eta}
is
\mathcal{A}
-bilinear and
\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}/\mathbb{F}_{f})
-equivariant.

(b)

If
\ker\eta
and
\ker\eta^{*}
have full dimension, then
[\cdot\mathbin{,}\cdot]_{\eta}
is non-degenerate.

Proof.

We will prove
\mathcal{A}
-bilinearity momentarily, but given that, Galois equivariance (with trivial action on
(\mathcal{A}/h\mathcal{A})^{\wedge}
) is straightforward from Proposition
3.6.5
(b). Also, in the situation of (b), if
\mathbf{x}\in\ker\eta
and
[\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}]_{\eta}=0
for all
\mathbf{y}\in\ker\eta^{*}
, then for all
a\in\mathcal{A}/h\mathcal{A}
,

0=[\mathbf{x}\mathbin{,}\mathbf{y}]_{\eta}(a)=\langle\mathcal{E}_{a}(\mathbf{x})\mathbin{,}\mathbf{y}\rangle_{\eta}.

The non-degeneracy of
\langle\cdot\mathbin{,}\cdot\rangle_{\eta}
from Proposition
3.6.5
(c) implies that
\mathcal{E}_{a}(\mathbf{x})=0
for all
a\in\mathcal{A}/h\mathcal{A}
. In particular for
a=1
, which implies
\mathbf{x}=0
. Likewise, for fixed
\mathbf{y}\in\ker\eta^{*}
, if
[\mathbf{x}\mathbin{,}\mathbf{y}]_{\eta}=0
for all
\mathbf{x}\in\ker\eta
, then using
\langle\mathcal{E}_{a}(\mathbf{x})\mathbin{,}\mathbf{y}\rangle_{\eta}=\langle\mathbf{x}\mathbin{,}\mathcal{E}_{a}^{*}(\mathbf{y})\rangle_{\eta}
from Proposition
3.6.9
, a similar argument implies that
\mathbf{y}=0
.

To prove the
\mathcal{A}
-bilinearity of
[\cdot\mathbin{,}\cdot]_{\eta}
, we note that it is
{\mathbb{F}_{q}\langle Z\rangle}
-bilinear, so we need only check that it respects multiplication by
\mathcal{A}
. For
\mathbf{x}\in\ker\eta
,
\mathbf{y}\in\ker\eta^{*}
, and
b\in\mathcal{A}
,

b\cdot[\mathbf{x}\mathbin{,}\mathbf{y}]_{\eta}=\bigl(a\mapsto\langle\mathcal{E}_{ba}(\mathbf{x})\mathbin{,}\mathbf{y}\rangle_{\eta}\bigr)

and

[\mathcal{E}_{b}(\mathbf{x})\mathbin{,}\mathbf{y}]_{\eta}=\bigl(a\mapsto\langle\mathcal{E}_{a}(\mathcal{E}_{b}(\mathbf{x}))\mathbin{,}\mathbf{y}\rangle_{\eta}\bigr).

Since
\mathcal{E}_{a}\mathcal{E}_{b}=\mathcal{E}_{ba}
, we have
b\cdot[\mathbf{x}\mathbin{,}\mathbf{y}]_{\eta}=[\mathcal{E}_{b}(\mathbf{x})\mathbin{,}\mathbf{y}]_{\eta}
. Similarly, since Proposition
3.6.9
implies
\langle\mathcal{E}_{a}(\mathbf{x})\mathbin{,}\mathcal{E}_{b}^{*}(\mathbf{y})\rangle_{\eta}=\langle\mathcal{E}_{b}(\mathcal{E}_{a}(\mathbf{x}))\mathbin{,}\mathbf{y}\rangle
, we have

[\mathbf{x}\mathbin{,}\mathcal{E}_{b}^{*}(\mathbf{y})]_{\eta}=\bigl(a\mapsto\langle\mathcal{E}_{b}(\mathcal{E}_{a}(\mathbf{x}))\mathbin{,}\mathbf{y}\rangle_{\eta}\bigr),

and so
b\cdot[\mathbf{x}\mathbin{,}\mathbf{y}]_{\eta}=[\mathbf{x}\mathbin{,}\mathcal{E}_{b}^{*}(\mathbf{y})]_{\eta}
as well.
∎

Corollary 3.6.12
.

Let
\mathcal{E}:\mathcal{A}\to\Mat_{\ell}({\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}[\tau])
be a
t
-module, and let
\eta:\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{E}
be an endomorphism over
{\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}
with
\det(\partial\eta)\neq 0
. If
\ker\eta
,
\ker\eta^{*}
have full dimension, then

\ker\eta\cong\ker\eta^{*}

as
\mathcal{A}
-modules and
\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}/\mathbb{F}_{f})
-modules. In particular as
\mathcal{A}
-modules,

\mathcal{E}({\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle})\cong\mathcal{E}^{*}({\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}).

Proof.

Let
h={[\ker\eta]}_{\mathcal{A}}
. The non-degeneracy of
[\cdot\mathbin{,}\cdot]_{\eta}
implies that we have isomorphisms of
\mathcal{A}
-modules,
\ker\eta^{*}\cong\Hom_{\mathcal{A}}(\ker\eta,(\mathcal{A}/h\mathcal{A})^{\wedge})\cong\Hom_{\mathcal{A}}(\ker\eta,\mathcal{A}/h\mathcal{A})
.
This last module is non-canonically isomorphic to
\ker\eta
, and by Proposition
3.6.11
(a), these maps respect the Galois action. The second part follows by taking
\eta=(1-\tau^{d})\mathrm{I}_{\ell}
from §
3.6.7
.
∎

3.6.13.
Applications to Tate modules

Let
\lambda\in\mathsf{A}_{+}
be irreducible, such that
\lambda(\theta)\neq f
. Then
\lambda
is also irreducible in
\mathcal{A}
, and
\det(\partial\mathcal{E}_{\lambda})\neq 0
. For
m\geqslant 1
, we write

(3.6.14)

\displaystyle\langle\cdot\mathbin{,}\cdot\rangle_{m}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\langle\cdot\mathbin{,}\cdot\rangle_{\mathcal{E}_{\lambda^{m}}}:\mathcal{E}[\lambda^{m}]\times\mathcal{E}^{*}[\lambda^{m}]

\displaystyle\to{\mathbb{F}_{q}\langle Z\rangle},

\displaystyle[\cdot\mathbin{,}\cdot]_{m}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=[\cdot\mathbin{,}\cdot]_{\mathcal{E}_{\lambda^{m}}}:\mathcal{E}[\lambda^{m}]\times\mathcal{E}^{*}[\lambda^{m}]

\displaystyle\to(\mathcal{A}/\lambda^{m}\mathcal{A})^{\wedge}.

In a similar fashion to the proof of Proposition
3.6.9
(cf.
[
44
]
*pp. 132–133
[
63
]
*Prop. 21, we find for all
m\geqslant 1
,
\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{E}[\lambda^{m+1}]
, and
\mathbf{y}\in\mathcal{E}^{*}[\lambda^{m}]
,

(3.6.15)

\langle\mathcal{E}_{\lambda}(\mathbf{x})\mathbin{,}\mathbf{y}\rangle_{m}=\langle\mathbf{x}\mathbin{,}\mathbf{y}\rangle_{m+1},\quad[\mathcal{E}_{\lambda}(\mathbf{x})\mathbin{,}\mathbf{y}]_{m}=[\mathbf{x}\mathbin{,}\mathbf{y}]_{m+1}.

These lead to the following result.

Proposition 3.6.16

(cf.
[
44
]
*Cor. 4.14.17
[
63
]
*Cor. 11)
.

Let
\mathcal{E}:\mathcal{A}\to\Mat_{\ell}({\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}[\tau])
be an Anderson
t
-module over
{\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}
, and let
\lambda\in\mathsf{A}_{+}
be irreducible with
\lambda(\theta)\neq f
. For each
m\geqslant 1
,
\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{E}[\lambda^{m+1}]
, and
\mathbf{y}\in\mathcal{E}^{*}[\lambda^{m+1}]
, we have

[\mathcal{E}_{\lambda}(\mathbf{x})\mathbin{,}\mathcal{E}_{\lambda}^{*}(\mathbf{y})]_{m}=\lambda\cdot[\mathbf{x}\mathbin{,}\mathbf{y}]_{m+1}.

Proof.

By (
3.6.15
), we have
[\mathcal{E}_{\lambda}(\mathbf{x})\mathbin{,}\mathcal{E}_{\lambda}^{*}(\mathbf{y})]_{m}=[\mathbf{x}\mathbin{,}\mathcal{E}_{\lambda}^{*}(\mathbf{y})]_{m+1}
, which then is the same as
\lambda\cdot[\mathbf{x}\mathbin{,}\mathbf{y}]_{m+1}
by
\mathcal{A}
-bilinearity.
∎

Definition 3.6.17
.

The
\lambda
-adic
Tate modules
,

T_{\lambda}(\mathcal{E})\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\varprojlim\mathcal{E}[\lambda^{m}],\quad T_{\lambda}(\mathcal{E}^{*})\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\varprojlim\mathcal{E}^{*}[\lambda^{m}],

are modules over
\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\varprojlim\mathcal{A}/\lambda^{m}\mathcal{A}
. When

(a)

\mathcal{E}
is abelian with
\rank_{{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}[t]}\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{E}}({\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}\langle Z\rangle})=r
,

(b)

\mathcal{E}
is
\mathcal{A}
-finite with
\rank_{{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}[t]}\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{E}}({\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}\langle Z\rangle})=r
,

(c)

\mathcal{E}[\lambda^{m}]
and
\mathcal{E}^{*}[\lambda^{m}]
have full dimension for each
m\geqslant 1
,

Corollary
3.3.8
implies that

(3.6.18)

T_{\lambda}(\mathcal{E})\cong\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}^{r},\quad T_{\lambda}(\mathcal{E}^{*})\cong\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}^{r}.

As usual
T_{\lambda}(\mathcal{E})
and
T_{\lambda}(\mathcal{E}^{*})
are
\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}/\mathbb{F}_{f})
-modules.
By Proposition
3.6.16
, the pairings in (
3.6.14
) fit together to induce a pairing on Tate modules.

Theorem 3.6.19

(cf.
[
44
]
*Thm. 4.14.20
[
63
]
*Prop. 21)
.

Let
\mathcal{E}:\mathcal{A}\to\Mat_{\ell}({\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}[\tau])
be an Anderson
t
-module over
{\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}
, and let
\lambda\in\mathsf{A}_{+}
be irreducible with
\lambda(\theta)\neq f
.
We have a continuous
\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}
-bilinear pairing,

[\cdot\mathbin{,}\cdot]_{\lambda}:T_{\lambda}(\mathcal{E})\times T_{\lambda}(\mathcal{E}^{*})\to\mathcal{A}_{\lambda},

with the following properties.

(a)

[\cdot\mathbin{,}\cdot]_{\lambda}
is
\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}/\mathbb{F}_{f})
-equivariant, where the Galois action on
\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}
is trivial.

(b)

If conditions (a)–(c) from Definition
3.6.17
hold, then
[\cdot\mathbin{,}\cdot]_{\lambda}
is non-degenerate.

Proof.

We first observe that
\varprojlim(\mathcal{A}/\lambda^{m}\mathcal{A})^{\wedge}\cong\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}
(e.g., see
[
44
]
*Prop. 4.14.19). The
\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}
-bilinearity follows from Proposition
3.6.16
, and Galois equivariance follows from Proposition
3.6.11
(b). Under the conditions of Definition
3.6.17
(a)–(c), Corollary
3.3.8
and Proposition
3.6.11
(c) imply non-degeneracy.
∎

One consequence of this theorem is that under conditions (a)–(c) of Definition
3.6.17
,

(3.6.20)

T_{\lambda}(\mathcal{E}^{*})\cong T_{\lambda}(\mathcal{E})^{\vee}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\Hom_{\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}}(T_{\lambda}(\mathcal{E}),\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}),

as
\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}/\mathbb{F}_{f})
-modules. In particular the Galois representations on
T_{\lambda}(\mathcal{E})
and
T_{\lambda}(\mathcal{E}^{*})
are dual to each other.

Remark 3.6.21
.

Other pairings on
t
-module torsion over finite fields have been developed by Taguchi
[
68
]
using a construction of duals for
t
-modules instead of adjoint
t
-modules. His pairings differ from
[\cdot\mathbin{,}\cdot]_{\lambda}
in that, like the Weil pairing, they take values in
T_{\lambda}(\mathsf{C})
where
\mathsf{C}
is the Carlitz module (see
[
68
]
*Thm. 4.3). For an adaptation of Taguchi’s methods to the pairing
[\cdot\mathbin{,}\cdot]_{\lambda}
for Drinfeld modules, see Taguchi’s appendix to Goss
[
43
]
.

3.7.
Characteristic polynomials and
\mathcal{A}
-orders

We now fix a
t
-module

\mathcal{E}:\mathcal{A}\to\Mat_{\ell}({\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}[\tau]),

and let
\lambda\in\mathsf{A}_{+}
be irreducible with
\lambda(\theta)\neq f
. We assume that
\mathcal{E}
satistifies Definition
3.6.17
(a)–(c).
Then
\tau^{d}\cdot\mathrm{I}_{\ell}:\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{E}
induces an automorphism of
T_{\lambda}(\mathcal{E})
, which coincides with the action of the
q^{d}
-th power Frobenius
\alpha\in\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}/\mathbb{F}_{f})
. Likewise,
\sigma^{d}\cdot\mathrm{I}_{\ell}:\mathcal{E}^{*}\to\mathcal{E}^{*}
induces an automorphism of
T_{\lambda}(\mathcal{E}^{*})
that coincides with
\alpha^{-1}
. If
B\in\GL_{r}(\mathcal{A}_{\lambda})
represents the action of
\tau^{d}
on
T_{\lambda}(\mathcal{E})
, then
(B^{-1})^{{\mathsf{T}}}
represents the action of
\tau^{d}
on
T_{\lambda}(\mathcal{E})^{\vee}
. By (
3.6.20
), we thus have equalities of characteristic polynomials,

(3.7.1)

\displaystyle\Char(\tau^{d},T_{\lambda}(\mathcal{E}),X)

\displaystyle=\Char(\sigma^{d},T_{\lambda}(\mathcal{E}^{*}),X),

\displaystyle\Char(\sigma^{d},T_{\lambda}(\mathcal{E}),X)

\displaystyle=\Char(\tau^{d},T_{\lambda}(\mathcal{E}^{*}),X).

Now choose
\mathbf{m}\in\Mat_{r\times 1}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{E}}({\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}))
and
\mathbf{n}\in\Mat_{r\times 1}(\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{E}}({\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}))
that are
{\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}[t]
-bases as in §
3.5
, together with
\Gamma
,
\Phi\in\Mat_{r}({\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}[t])
so that
\tau\mathbf{m}=\Gamma\mathbf{m}
,
\sigma\mathbf{n}=\Phi\mathbf{n}
. Finally, fix
\mathrm{G}
,
\mathrm{H}\in\Mat_{r}({\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}[t])
as in (
3.5.3
).

For simplicity we temporarily abbreviate
\mathcal{M}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{E}}({\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}\langle Z\rangle})
, and for an endomorphism
\eta:\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{E}
we write

\biggl(\frac{\mathcal{M}}{\mathcal{M}\eta}\biggr)^{{\tau=\mathrm{id}}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\ker\biggl(\frac{\mathcal{M}}{\mathcal{M}\eta}\xrightarrow{(\tau-1)(\cdot)}\frac{\mathcal{M}}{\mathcal{M}\eta}\biggr).

We recall the
{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}[t]
-linear isomorphism
\mathbf{i}:\Mat_{1\times r}({\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}[t])\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathcal{M}
from §
3.5
.

Proposition 3.7.2
.

Let
m\geqslant 1
. The following diagram of
\mathcal{A}
-modules commutes:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}^{*}[\lambda^{m}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\biggl(\dfrac{\mathcal{M}}{\lambda^{m}\mathcal{M}}\biggr)^{{\tau=\mathrm{id}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\biggl(\dfrac{\Mat_{1\times r}({\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}[t])}{\lambda^{m}\Mat_{1\times r}({\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}[t])}\biggr)^{{\tau=\mathrm{id}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}^{*}[\lambda^{m}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\biggl(\dfrac{\mathcal{M}}{\lambda^{m}\mathcal{M}}\biggr)^{{\tau=\mathrm{id}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\biggl(\dfrac{\Mat_{1\times r}({\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}[t])}{\lambda^{m}\Mat_{1\times r}({\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}[t])}\biggr)^{{\tau=\mathrm{id}}},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sigma^{d}(\cdot)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\cdot)\tau^{d}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\cdot)\mathrm{G}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

where the first set of horizontal isomorphisms are from (
3.3.5
).

Proof.

From (
3.2.4
) the map on
\mathcal{M}
induced by
\sigma^{d}\cdot\mathrm{I}_{\ell}:\mathcal{E}^{*}\to\mathcal{E}^{*}
is right-multiplication by
\tau^{d}
. Noting that elements of
(\mathcal{M}/\lambda^{m}\mathcal{M})^{{\tau=\mathrm{id}}}
are classes fixed under
left
-multiplication by
\tau-1
, it follows quickly that right-multiplication by
\tau^{d}
on
(\mathcal{M}/\lambda^{m}\mathcal{M})^{{\tau=\mathrm{id}}}
is a well-defined
\mathcal{A}
-module map. Winding through the snake lemma applied to (
3.2.4
), we see that the horizontal maps are defined as follows: for
\mathbf{y}\in\mathcal{E}^{*}[\lambda^{m}]
, there exist
\beta
,
\beta^{\prime}\in\mathcal{M}
so that
\gamma_{1}(\beta)=\mathbf{y}
and
\lambda^{m}\cdot\beta=(\tau-1)\beta^{\prime}
, and then

\mathbf{y}\mapsto\beta^{\prime}+\lambda^{m}\mathcal{M}
.
But then
\gamma_{1}(\beta\tau^{d})=\mathbf{y}^{(-d)}=\sigma^{d}(\mathbf{y})
and
\lambda^{m}\cdot\beta\tau^{d}=(\tau-1)\beta^{\prime}\tau^{d}
, and so
\sigma^{d}(\mathbf{y})=\beta^{\prime}\tau^{d}+\lambda^{m}\mathcal{M}
as desired. This verifies the commutatitivity of the first square.

Lemma
3.5.4
implies that the second square commutes without each term being fixed by left-multiplication by
\tau-1
. It suffices to show the right-most vertical map is well-defined. For
\mathbf{u}\in\Mat_{1\times r}({\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}[t])
such that
(\tau-1)\mathbf{u}\in\lambda^{m}\Mat_{1\times r}({\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}[t])
, we have by definition of the
\tau
-action on
\Mat_{1\times r}({\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}[t])
through
\mathbf{i}
that for some
\mathbf{v}\in\Mat_{1\times r}({\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}[t])
,

(\tau-1)\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{u}^{(1)}\Gamma-\mathbf{u}=\lambda^{m}\mathbf{v}.

Recalling that
\mathrm{G}=\Gamma^{(d-1)}\cdots\Gamma^{(1)}\Gamma
, we see that

(\tau-1)\mathbf{u}\mathrm{G}=\bigl(\mathbf{u}^{(1)}\mathrm{G}^{(1)}\Gamma-\mathbf{u}\mathrm{G}\bigr)=\bigl(\mathbf{u}^{(1)}\Gamma^{(d)}-\mathbf{u}\bigr)\mathrm{G}.

Since
\Gamma\in\Mat_{r}({\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}[t])
, we have
\Gamma^{(d)}=\Gamma
, and so
(\tau-1)\mathbf{u}\mathrm{G}=\lambda^{m}\mathbf{v}\mathrm{G}
as desired.
∎

Corollary 3.7.3
.

Let
\mathcal{E}:\mathcal{A}\to\Mat_{\ell}({\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}[\tau])

be a
t
-module defined over
{\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}
. Let
\lambda\in\mathsf{A}_{+}
be irreducible with
\lambda(\theta)\neq f
such that Definition
3.6.17
(a)–(c) are satisfied.
For
\mathrm{G}=\Gamma^{(d-1)}\cdots\Gamma^{(1)}\Gamma\in\Mat_{r}({\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}[t])
, where
\Gamma
represents multiplication by
\tau
on
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{E}}({\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle})
, we have

\Char(\tau^{d},T_{\lambda}(\mathcal{E}),X)=\Char(\mathrm{G},X).

Furthermore, this polynomial is in
\mathcal{A}[X]={\mathbb{F}_{q}\langle Z\rangle}[t,X]
and is independent of the choice of
\lambda
for which Definition
3.6.17
(c) is satisfied.

Proof.

For each
m\geqslant 1
, because
\mathcal{E}[\lambda^{m}]
and
\mathcal{E}^{*}[\lambda^{m}]
have full dimension, we see from Corollary
3.3.8
that each is isomorphic to
(\mathcal{A}/\lambda^{m}\mathcal{A})^{r}
. By the proof of Proposition
3.3.4
, there is an
\mathcal{A}/\lambda^{m}\mathcal{A}
-basis of
(\mathcal{M}/\lambda^{m}\mathcal{M})^{{\tau=\mathrm{id}}}
of rank
r
that is also an
({\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}[t]/\lambda^{m}{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}[t])
-basis of
\mathcal{M}/\lambda^{m}\mathcal{M}
. Passing this basis to the right-hand column of Proposition
3.7.2
, we obtain an
{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}[t]
-basis
\mathbf{u}_{1},\dots,\mathbf{u}_{r}\in\Mat_{1\times r}({\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}[t])
, such that their images

\mathbf{u}_{1},\dots,\mathbf{u}_{r}\in\biggl(\dfrac{\Mat_{1\times r}({\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}[t])}{\lambda^{m}\Mat_{1\times r}({\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}[t])}\biggr)^{{\tau=\mathrm{id}}}

form an
\mathcal{A}/\lambda^{m}\mathcal{A}
-basis. If we let
U=(\mathbf{u}_{1},\dots,\mathbf{u}_{r})^{{\mathsf{T}}}\in\GL_{r}({\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}[t])
, then
U\mathrm{G}U^{-1}
represents the right-hand vertical map in Proposition
3.7.2
with respect to this basis.
Therefore, Proposition
3.7.2
implies that the characteristic polynomial of
\sigma^{d}
acting on the
\mathcal{A}/\lambda^{m}\mathcal{A}
-module
\mathcal{E}^{*}[\lambda^{m}]
is

\Char(\sigma^{d},\mathcal{E}^{*}[\lambda^{m}],X)=\Char(U\mathrm{G}U^{-1},X)=\Char(\mathrm{G},X)\bmod\lambda^{m}.

Passing the diagram in Proposition
3.7.2
to the inverse limit, we obtain

\Char(\tau^{d},T_{\lambda}(\mathcal{E}),X)=\Char(\sigma^{d},T_{\lambda}(\mathcal{E}^{*}),X)=\Char(\mathrm{G},X)\in{\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}[t],

where the first equality is (
3.7.1
). Since
\mathrm{G}
is independent of the choice of
\lambda
, it remains to verify that
\Char(\mathrm{G},X)\in\mathcal{A}[X]
. But using that
\Gamma^{(d)}=\Gamma
and that
\Char(BC,X)=\Char(CB,X)
for matrices
B
and
C
,

\Char(\mathrm{G},X)^{(1)}=\Char(\mathrm{G}^{(1)},X)=\Char(\Gamma^{(d)}\cdots\Gamma^{(1)},X)\\
=\Char(\Gamma^{(d-1)}\cdots\Gamma^{(1)}\Gamma^{(d)},X)=\Char(\mathrm{G},X),

and so
\Char(\mathrm{G},X)\in\mathcal{A}[X]
.
∎

This corollary can be used to find characteristic polynomials of Frobenius acting on Tate modules for global
L
-functions, and moreover to calculate
\mu_{\phi}(a)
and
\nu_{\phi}(a)
, as defined in §
2.3
. See Remark
5.1.2
.

We can also use Corollary
3.7.3
to determine
{[\mathcal{E}({\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle})]}_{\mathcal{A}}
. For Drinfeld modules over finite fields, this was found by Gekeler
[
31
]
*Thm. 5.1(i) using methods of Deuring involving reduced norms on quaternion endomorphism algebras, techniques which were not available in our current setting. Furthermore, the methods of Yu
[
44
]
*Prop. 4.12.21
[
73
]
do not readily extend to Anderson
\mathcal{A}
-modules, as we do not have as fine control over
\nu
-torsion when
\nu\in\mathcal{A}\setminus A
.
However, the results of the previous sections yield the following. We note that this result extends Gekeler’s result both to certain
t
-modules over finite fields (using
Z=\emptyset
) but also over general
{\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}
.

Theorem 3.7.4
.

Let
\mathcal{E}:\mathcal{A}\to\Mat_{\ell}({\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}[\tau])
be a
t
-module defined over
{\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}
. Let
\lambda\in\mathsf{A}_{+}
be irreducible with
\lambda(\theta)\neq f
such that Definition
3.6.17
(a)–(c) are satisfied. Then

\bigl[\mathcal{E}({\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle})\bigr]_{\mathcal{A}}=\gamma\cdot\Char(\tau^{d},T_{\lambda}(\mathcal{E}),1),

where
\gamma\in{\mathbb{F}_{q}\langle Z\rangle}^{\times}
uniquely forces the right-hand expression to be monic in
t
.

Proof.

We let
M\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\Mat_{1\times r}({\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}[t])
. By Lemma
3.5.5
(a), we have

(3.7.5)

\mathcal{E}^{*}({\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle})\cong\biggl(\frac{M}{M(\mathrm{I}-\mathrm{G})}\biggr)^{{\tau=\mathrm{id}}}.

Because
\mathcal{E}^{*}({\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle})
has full dimension, as in the proof of Proposition
3.3.4
we can find an
{\mathbb{F}_{q}\langle Z\rangle}
-basis of
(M/M(\mathrm{I}-\mathrm{G}))^{{\tau=\mathrm{id}}}
that is also an
{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}
-basis of
M/M(\mathrm{I}-\mathrm{G})
. Therefore,

{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}\otimes_{{\mathbb{F}_{q}\langle Z\rangle}}\biggl(\frac{M}{M(\mathrm{I}-\mathrm{G})}\biggr)^{{\tau=\mathrm{id}}}\cong\frac{M}{M(\mathrm{I}-\mathrm{G})}.

A priori this is an isomorphism of
{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}
-vector spaces, but it is also an isomorphism of
{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}[t]
-modules. Since the left-hand side is the extension of scalars of a finitely generated torsion
\mathcal{A}
-module, it follows that

(3.7.6)

\biggl[\biggl(\frac{M}{M(\mathrm{I}-\mathrm{G})}\biggr)^{{\tau=\mathrm{id}}}\,\biggr]_{\mathcal{A}}={\left[\frac{M}{M(\mathrm{I}-\mathrm{G})}\right]}_{{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}[t]}=\gamma\cdot\det(\mathrm{I}-\mathrm{G}),\quad\gamma\in{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{f}\langle Z\rangle}^{\times},

where
\gamma
is uniquely chosen to make
\gamma\cdot\det(\mathrm{I}-\mathrm{G})
monic in
t
. On the other hand, Corollary
3.7.3
shows that

\Char(\tau^{d},T_{\lambda}(\mathcal{E}),1)=\det(\mathrm{I}-\mathrm{G})\in\mathcal{A},

so
\gamma\in{\mathbb{F}_{q}\langle Z\rangle}^{\times}
, and thus also by (
3.7.5
),

(3.7.7)

\bigl[\mathcal{E}^{*}({\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle})\bigr]_{\mathcal{A}}=\gamma\cdot\Char(\tau^{d},T_{\lambda}(\mathcal{E}),1).

Since Corollary
3.6.12
implies
{[\mathcal{E}^{*}({\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle})]}_{\mathcal{A}}={[\mathcal{E}({\mathbb{F}_{f}\langle Z\rangle})]}_{\mathcal{A}}
, we are done.
∎

We note that if
Z=\emptyset
and
E
is a
t
-module over
\mathbb{F}_{f}
that is abelian and
\mathsf{A}
-finite, then Definition
3.6.17
(a)–(c) are automatically satisfied. Indeed (a) and (b) are assured by Maurischat
[
52
]
, and (c) follows from Anderson
[
4
]
*Prop. 1.8.3
[
44
]
*Cor. 5.6.4. We thus have the following corollary for
t
-modules over finite fields.

Corollary 3.7.8
.

Let
E:\mathsf{A}\to\Mat_{\ell}(\mathbb{F}_{f}[\tau])
be an abelian and
\mathsf{A}
-finite
t
-module defined over
\mathbb{F}_{f}
. Let
\lambda\in\mathsf{A}_{+}
be irreducible with
\lambda(\theta)\neq f
. Then

\bigl[E(\mathbb{F}_{f})\bigr]_{\mathsf{A}}=\gamma\cdot\Char(\tau^{d},T_{\lambda}(E),1),

where
\gamma\in\mathbb{F}_{q}^{\times}
forces the right-hand expression to be monic in
t
.

4.
Rigid analytic twists of Drinfeld modules

The main objects of study of the present paper are Anderson
t
-modules over
\widehat{\mathbb{L}}_{z}
that are obtained by conjugating a Drinfeld module over
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
by the rigid analytic trivialization of another. In particular we focus on the case that both Drinfeld modules are defined over
A
and have everywhere good reduction. These constructions were inspired by Anglès, Pellarin, and Tavares Ribeiro
[
8
]
, who investigated the case of conjugating the Carlitz module by the Anderson-Thakur function
\omega_{z}
, and by Anglès and Tavares Ribeiro
[
13
]
and Gezmiş
[
35
]
, who studied the case of conjugating a Drinfeld module by
\omega_{z}
.

4.1.
Properties of
\mathbb{E}(\phi\times\psi)

We start with two Drinfeld modules
\phi
,
\psi:\mathsf{A}\to A[\tau]
, defined over
A
with everywhere good reduction, such that

(4.1.1)

\displaystyle\phi_{t}
