\displaystyle=\sum_{a_{1},a_{2}\in A_{+}}\frac{\overline{\chi}_{\psi}(a_{2})a_{2}(z)\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\phi,\theta}(a_{1},a_{2})\nu_{\psi,z}(a_{1})}{a_{1}a_{2}}

\displaystyle=1-\theta^{-1}-z\theta^{-2}-\theta^{-3}+O(\theta^{-4}).

This agrees numerically with
\det(\Upsilon_{\psi,z}^{-1}\Log_{\phi}(\Upsilon_{\psi,z}))
, as expected from Corollary
6.3.6
(bc).

Example 6.4.3
.

(
r=\ell=3
). Let
\phi
,
\psi:\mathbb{F}_{3}[t]\to\mathbb{F}_{3}[\theta][\tau]
be given by

\phi_{t}=\theta+\theta^{2}\tau+\tau^{2}+\tau^{3},\quad\psi_{t}=\theta+\tau+\tau^{2}+\tau^{3},

so that for
\mathbb{E}=\mathbb{E}(\phi\times\psi)
we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{t}=\begin{pmatrix}\theta&0&0\\
0&\theta&0\\
0&0&\theta\end{pmatrix}+\theta^{2}\begin{pmatrix}0&0&z-\theta\\
1&0&-1\\
0&1&-1\end{pmatrix}\tau

\displaystyle{}+\begin{pmatrix}0&z-\theta&-z+\theta\\
0&-1&z+1-\theta^{3}\\
1&-1&0\end{pmatrix}\tau^{2}

\displaystyle{}+\begin{pmatrix}z-\theta&-z+\theta&0\\
-1&z+1-\theta^{3}&-z+\theta^{3}\\
-1&0&z+1-\theta^{9}\end{pmatrix}\tau^{3}.

Here
[
49
]
*Eq. (2.11) implies that
R_{\phi}=\lvert\theta\rvert^{1/2}
, and we find from
[
49
]
*Thm. 4.4 that
\lVert\Upsilon_{\psi,z}\rVert=\lvert\theta\rvert^{9/26}<R_{\phi}
. We calculate

\displaystyle L(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\phi,\theta}\times\boldsymbol{\nu}_{\psi,z},0)

\displaystyle=\sum_{a_{1},a_{2}\in A_{+}}\frac{\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\phi,\theta}(a_{1},a_{2})\boldsymbol{\nu}_{\psi,z}(a_{1},a_{2})}{a_{1}a_{2}}

\displaystyle=1+\theta^{-1}+\theta^{-2}+(1-z)\theta^{-3}+O(\theta^{-4}),

which agrees numerically with the expected value
(\theta-z)\cdot\omega_{z}/\widetilde{\pi}\cdot\det(\Upsilon_{\psi,z}^{-1}\Log_{\phi}(\Upsilon_{\psi,z}))
from Corollary
6.2.4
(b).

6.4.4.
The case
\lVert\Upsilon_{\psi,z}\rVert\geqslant R_{\phi}

Suppose that
\mathbb{E}=\mathbb{E}(\phi\times\psi)
has been chosen with
\phi
,
\psi:\mathsf{A}\to A[\tau]
of ranks
r
and
\ell
respectively. Further suppose that
\lVert\Upsilon_{\psi,z}\rVert\geqslant R_{\phi}
. Then the identities in Corollaries
6.2.4
(b) and
6.3.6
(c) do not hold, but one can approach determining
\mathrm{Reg}_{\mathbb{E}}
by using ideas of Demeslay
[
24
]
*Prop. 2.6 and Taelman
[
66
]
*Thm. 1.

Defining the polydisc
\mathrm{D}(\mathbb{K}_{\infty})\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\{\mathbf{z}\in\mathbb{K}_{\infty}^{\ell}\mid\lVert\mathbf{z}\rVert<\lVert\Upsilon_{\psi,z}\rVert^{-1}\cdot R_{\phi}\}
, we find that
\mathrm{D}(\mathbb{K}_{\infty})
is contained within the domain of convergence of
\Log_{\mathbb{E}}=\Upsilon_{\psi,z}^{-1}\cdot\Log_{\phi}\cdot\Upsilon_{\psi,z}
and that (
3.4.7
) applies. The exponential induces a surjective map of
\mathbb{F}_{q}(z)
-vector spaces,

(6.4.5)

\mathbb{K}_{\infty}^{\ell}\xrightarrow{\Exp_{\mathbb{E},\mathbb{K}_{\infty}}}\frac{\mathbb{E}(\mathbb{K}_{\infty})}{\mathbb{E}(\mathbb{A})+\mathrm{D}(\mathbb{K}_{\infty})}.

As
\mathbb{K}_{\infty}={\mathbb{F}_{q}(z)(\!(\theta^{-1})\!)}
, it follows that the right-hand quotient in (
6.4.5
) is finite dimensional over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(z)
: indeed, if we let
j_{0}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\log_{q}(\lVert\Upsilon_{\psi,z}\rVert^{-1}\cdot R_{\phi})\in\mathbb{R}
, then every vector in the right-hand space has entries in the
\mathbb{F}_{q}(z)
-linear span of
\{\theta^{j}\mid j\in\mathbb{Z},\ j_{0}\leqslant j\leqslant-1\}
modulo
\mathbb{E}(\mathbb{A})+\mathrm{D}(\mathbb{K}_{\infty})
. (We note that
\lVert\Upsilon_{\psi,z}\rVert<R_{\phi}
implies that
j_{0}>0
, in which case
\mathbb{E}(\mathbb{A})+\mathrm{D}(\mathbb{K}_{\infty})=\mathbb{E}(\mathbb{K}_{\infty})
, and moreover the standard basis vectors are in
\mathbb{E}(\mathbb{A})\cap\mathrm{D}(\mathbb{K}_{\infty})
.)

Since
\mathbb{K}_{\infty}
is infinite dimensional over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(z)
, we can find
\mathbf{x}_{1},\dots,\mathbf{x}_{\ell}\in\mathbb{K}_{\infty}^{\ell}
so that

\Exp_{\mathbb{E}}(\mathbf{x}_{1}),\ldots,\Exp_{\mathbb{E}}(\mathbf{x}_{\ell})\in\mathbb{E}(\mathbb{A})+\mathrm{D}(\mathbb{K}_{\infty}).

For each
\mathbf{x}_{i}
, write
\Exp_{\mathbb{E}}(\mathbf{x}_{i})=\mathbf{b}_{i}+\mathbf{y}_{i}
with
\mathbf{b}_{i}\in\mathbb{E}(\mathbb{A})
and
\mathbf{y}_{i}\in\mathrm{D}(\mathbb{K}_{\infty})
.
Then forming
\ell\times\ell
matrices
\mathrm{X}=(\mathbf{x}_{i})
,
\mathrm{B}=(\mathbf{b}_{i})
, and
\mathrm{Y}=(\mathbf{y}_{i})
, we have
\Exp_{\mathbb{E},\mathbb{K}_{\infty}}(\mathrm{X})=\mathrm{B}+\mathrm{Y}
, and

\mathrm{X}-\Log_{\mathbb{E}}(\mathrm{Y})\in\Mat_{1\times\ell}\bigl(\Exp_{\mathbb{E},\mathbb{K}_{\infty}}^{-1}\bigl(\mathbb{E}(\mathbb{A})\bigr)\bigr).

When chosen appropriately, the columns of
\mathrm{X}-\Log_{\mathbb{E}}(\mathrm{Y})
are
\mathbb{A}
-linearly independent (or equivalently
\mathbb{K}_{\infty}
-linearly independent since
\Exp_{\mathbb{E},\mathbb{K}_{\infty}}^{-1}(\mathbb{E}(\mathbb{A}))
is an
\mathbb{A}
-lattice) and their
\mathbb{A}
-span comprises a submodule of
\smash{\Exp_{\mathbb{E},\mathbb{K}_{\infty}}^{-1}}(\mathbb{E}(\mathbb{A}))
of finite
\mathbb{A}
-index, from which it is possible to determine
\smash{\Exp_{\mathbb{E},\mathbb{K}_{\infty}}^{-1}}(\mathbb{E}(\mathbb{A}))
in full on a case by case basis.

Example 6.4.6
.

Let
\phi:\mathbb{F}_{3}[t]\to\mathbb{F}_{3}[\theta][\tau]
be defined by

\phi_{t}=\theta+\theta^{3}\tau+\tau^{2}
,
and let
\mathbb{E}=\mathbb{E}(\phi\times\phi)
, so

\mathbb{E}_{t}=\begin{pmatrix}\theta&0\\
0&\theta\end{pmatrix}+\theta^{3}\begin{pmatrix}0&-\theta+z\\
1&-\theta^{3}\end{pmatrix}\tau+\begin{pmatrix}z-\theta&-z\theta^{9}+\theta^{10}\\
-\theta^{3}&z-\theta^{3}+\theta^{12}\end{pmatrix}\tau^{2}.

Similar to the previous examples, we compute that
R_{\phi}=1
and that
\lVert\Upsilon_{\psi,z}\rVert=\lvert\theta\rvert^{3/2}=3^{3/2}>1
. In this case
\mathrm{D}(\mathbb{K}_{\infty})=\{O(\theta^{-2})\}\subseteq\mathbb{K}_{\infty}^{2}
, and so
\mathbb{E}(\mathbb{K}_{\infty})/(E(\mathbb{A})+\mathrm{D}(\mathbb{K}_{\infty}))\cong\mathbb{F}_{q}(z)\cdot\theta^{-1}
.
By direct calculation we find

\displaystyle\Exp_{\mathbb{E}}\begin{pmatrix}1\\
0\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}1\\
1\end{pmatrix}+\begin{pmatrix}-\theta^{-5}+z\theta^{-6}-\theta^{-8}+z\theta^{-9}+\cdots\\
\theta^{-2}-\theta^{-3}+\theta^{-4}+\theta^{-8}-\theta^{-9}+\cdots\end{pmatrix},

\displaystyle\Exp_{\mathbb{E}}\begin{pmatrix}\theta^{-1}\\
0\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}1\\
0\end{pmatrix}\theta^{-1}+\begin{pmatrix}-\theta^{-14}+z\theta^{-15}+\cdots\\
\theta^{-3}+\theta^{-5}+\theta^{-7}+\cdots\end{pmatrix},

\displaystyle\Exp_{\mathbb{E}}\begin{pmatrix}\theta\\
0\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=\begin{aligned} \begin{pmatrix}\theta^{10}-z\theta^{9}-\theta^{4}+z\theta^{3}+z\\
\theta^{12}-\theta^{6}-\theta^{3}+\theta+z-1\end{pmatrix}&{}+\begin{pmatrix}0\\
1\end{pmatrix}\theta^{-1}\\
&{}+\begin{pmatrix}-\theta^{-2}+z\theta^{-3}-\theta^{-4}+\cdots\\
-\theta^{-2}+-\theta^{-3}+\theta^{-7}+\cdots\end{pmatrix},\end{aligned}

\displaystyle\Exp_{\mathbb{E}}\begin{pmatrix}0\\
1\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=\begin{aligned} \begin{pmatrix}-\theta^{10}+z\theta^{9}+\theta^{4}-z\theta^{3}\\
-\theta^{12}+\theta^{6}+\theta^{3}-\theta-z-1\end{pmatrix}&{}+\begin{pmatrix}-1\\
-1\end{pmatrix}\theta^{-1}\\
&\hskip-90.0pt{}+\begin{pmatrix}(z+1)\theta^{-2}-(z+1)\theta^{-3}+(z+1)\theta^{-4}+\cdots\\
\theta^{-2}+(z-1)\theta^{-6}-\theta^{-7}+\cdots\end{pmatrix}.\end{aligned}

Picking
\mathbf{x}_{1}=\bigl(\begin{smallmatrix}1\\
0\end{smallmatrix}\bigr)
and
\mathbf{x}_{2}=\bigl(\begin{smallmatrix}\theta+\theta^{-1}\\
1\end{smallmatrix}\bigr)
, we find that
\Exp_{\mathbb{E}}(\mathbf{x}_{1})
,
\Exp_{\mathbb{E}}(\mathbf{x}_{2})\in\mathbb{E}(\mathbb{A})+\mathrm{D}(\mathbb{K}_{\infty})
. Moreover, defining
\mathbf{y}_{1}
,
\mathbf{y}_{2}\in\mathrm{D}(\mathbb{K}_{\infty})
, as in the previous paragraph, we obtain

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{1}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\begin{pmatrix}1\\
0\end{pmatrix}-\Log_{\mathbb{E}}(\mathbf{y}_{1})=\begin{pmatrix}1-\theta^{-7}+(z+1)\theta^{-8}-(z+1)\theta^{-9}+\cdots\\
-\theta^{-2}-\theta^{-4}-\theta^{-5}-\theta^{-7}-\theta^{-8}-\theta^{-9}+\cdots\end{pmatrix},

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{2}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\begin{pmatrix}\theta+\theta^{-1}\\
1\end{pmatrix}-\Log_{\mathbb{E}}(\mathbf{y}_{2})

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}\theta+\theta^{-1}-z\theta^{-2}+\theta^{-3}-z\theta^{-4}+\theta^{-5}-z\theta^{-6}+\theta^{-7}-z\theta^{-8}+\theta^{-9}+\cdots\\
1-\theta^{-5}+\theta^{-6}-\theta^{-7}+z\theta^{-8}-(z+1)\theta^{-9}+\cdots\end{pmatrix},

and
\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{1}
,
\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{2}\in\Exp_{\mathbb{E},\mathbb{K}_{\infty}}^{-1}(\mathbb{E}(\mathbb{A}))
. Taking a determinant we obtain,

(6.4.7)

\det(\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{1},\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{2})=1+\theta^{-1}-\theta^{-3}+(-z+1)\theta^{-4}\\
{}+\theta^{-5}+z\theta^{-6}+(-z+1)\theta^{-7}+\theta^{-8}-(z+1)\theta^{-9}+O(\theta^{-10}).

Since the infinite product in Demeslay’s formula in Theorem
5.3.10
is a
1
-unit in
\mathbb{K}_{\infty}
, the fact that
\det(\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{1},\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{2})
is also a
1
-unit in
\mathbb{K}_{\infty}
implies that

\Exp_{\mathbb{E},\mathbb{K}_{\infty}}^{-1}\bigl(\mathbb{E}(\mathbb{A})\bigr)=\mathbb{A}\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{1}+\mathbb{A}\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{2}\quad\textup{and}\quad{[\mathrm{H}(\mathbb{E})]}_{\mathbb{A}}=1.

Finally, as in previous examples we can compute directly that

L(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\phi,\theta}\times\boldsymbol{\nu}_{\phi,z},0)=\sum_{a\in A_{+}}\frac{\mu_{\phi,\theta}(a)\nu_{\phi,z}(a)}{a}=1+\theta^{-1}-\theta^{-2}+(z+1)\theta^{-3}+\theta^{-4}\\
{}-(z+1)\theta^{-5}+(z^{2}-1)\theta^{-6}+(-z^{2}+z)\theta^{-8}+z^{3}\theta^{-9}+O(\theta^{-10}),

and after multiplying by
\widetilde{\pi}/((\theta-z)\omega_{z})
we find that it indeed agrees with
\mathrm{Reg}_{\mathbb{E}}=\det(\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{1},\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{2})
in (
6.4.7
) up to
O(\theta^{-10})
, as expected by Corollary
6.2.4
(a).
